Dermot Mulroney On How Filming ‘The Family Stone’ Sequel Feels Like Life Imitating Art: “So Moved”

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

More than 20 years later, The Family Stone sequel has become a real-life family reunion of sorts for the cast of the 2005 holiday movie.

Dermot Mulroney, who reprises his role as Everett Stone in the upcoming sequel The Families Stone, recently expressed that he’s “so moved to be reunited” with his “incredible, incredible” co-stars from the original Thomas Bezucha-written/directed film.

“Everybody’s had this remarkable career advancement and paths since we were all together,” he told Entertainment Weekly. “So we’re making a movie about a family returning all together for the first time in years and years, and we’re doing that in real life too, returning and reuniting with this incredible cast.”

Mulroney added of his onscreen brother, “Luke Wilson and I always had a real tight thing going, so that snapped right back into place.” Watch on Deadline

The Families Stone picks up 20 years after the death of Diane Keaton’s character, matriarch Sybil Stone — something that was depicted in the original film — as the Stone family again reunites for Christmas, only to face the season’s biggest surprise: their father (Craig T. Nelson) has unexpectedly invited the woman (Jean Smart) he’s fallen in love with to meet the family.

Following Keaton’s death at age 79 in October 2025, Bezucha revealed that he was writing the sequel, which was set at Searchlight in September and is now filming in New York. In addition to Mulroney, Nelson and Wilson, original cast members Claire Danes, Rachel McAdams, Sarah Jessica Parker, Tyrone Giordano, Elizabeth Reaser, Paul Schneider and Brian White are returning.

Chanel Chance Eau Splendide Is My Favorite of All the Chance Perfumes. Here’s Why I Love It So Much

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Allow me to make the case for wearing it year-round.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

My mom’s Chanel No.5, a fixture in our bathroom cabinet, was the first perfume I ever knew by name. It’s sophisticated and timeless, much like most of the other best-of-the-best Chanel fragrances that have been released since No.5’s 1921 debut. And while I have nothing but respect for the classics, I’ll admit that some of the brand’s more traditional floral fragrances aren’t necessarily what I reach for on a regular basis. I’m usually more interested in something a little fruitier, fresher, and more playful.

Enter Chanel Chance Eau Splendide, the purple-hued 2025 addition to the brand’s beloved Chance fragrance collection. With a sparkling raspberry opening, a heart of rose geranium, and a soft, musky base, it promises a bright, youthful take on the classic Chanel scent profile. And as someone who spends a considerable amount of time sniffing new perfumes for work, I know things are serious when they earn an immediate permanent spot on my vanity.

After attending the launch event for Eau Splendide last April, I didn’t intend to make it my new go-to... but that’s exactly what happened. I got an early sample, started wearing it, and simply noticed that I was reaching for it nearly everyday. I quietly fell in love with it as it sneakily became one of my signature scents. Now, after wearing it pretty consistently for a year and a half, here’s my honest review of Chanel Chance Eau Splendide Eau de Parfum, including what it actually smells like on skin, how it wears throughout the day, and whether it’s worth the luxury price tag. Ahead, everything to know before adding it to your fragrance collection.

First things first: Chanel Chance Eau Splendide is a fruity floral, with a much brighter opening than you might expect from a traditional Chanel perfume. I’m not exactly surprised that I love it so much, given that fruity florals make up about 90 percent of my perfume collection.

The fragrance features a raspberry top note that gives it a juicy, slightly tart fruity sweetness. But rather than leaning into the saccharine, sugary territory of some fruity perfumes, Eau Splendide then introduces rose geranium as a heart note, which adds a fresh, rosy, and surprising-but-subtle minty moment. To me, this is how you construct a perfect fruity floral: Before it gets too sweet (and for me, headache-y), some fresh floral notes balance everything out.

But that’s not all: As the fragrance dries down, you’ll experience base notes of iris, cedar, and white musk that make for a soft finish. To me, it results in a scent that feels elegant, feminine, and sophisticated—very Chanel—without being particularly heavy.

Splendide is a fresh and light scent, but don’t think that means it’s not staying put on your skin throughout the day. It usually lasts about four to six hours on me, so while it’s certainly not the longest-lasting fragrance I own, about four spritzes of it gets me through most of the day.

With so many great Chanel fragrances, how does this stand up against the rest? If I had to describe the overall vibe, I’d say that Chance Eau Splendide makes me feel effortlessly put together, but it also has a playful streak to it. I smell the raspberry most prominently on my skin, though, as it gets later in the day, I also notice the iris and musk. Even though I have dozens of fruity floral perfumes, it’s different than any of the others in my fragrance wardrobe.

The first time I spritzed it, I did wonder if it was going to be too sweet for me. Ultimately, I found that the raspberry and rose geranium mix well together and don’t feel too sugary whatsoever. I also love how youthful it is, especially compared to some of the other Chanel fragrances out there. It’s super flirty, vibrant, and fresh, making it the perfect companion for daytime meetings, date nights, and more—I even wore it to Coachella this year. All in all, the one word I’d use to describe it is approachable. Is it the most complex perfume I’ve ever sniffed? No. But the scent, the vibe, and the longevity make it perfect for anyone from a first-time Chanel fragrance wearer (I think it’s a great introduction to the brand) to a fruity floral fanatic. For me, it’s an everyday bottle that takes me from day to night all year long.

On that last point: I can see why someone might call it a spring or summer perfume, given the notes. But there’s no rule that says you have to put away your fruity florals when temperatures drop. This is the kind of fragrance that works whenever I’m craving something bright and fresh.

Okay, so is Chanel Chance Eau Splendide actually worth the splurge? It’ll definitely be a re-purchase for me, and not just because it comes in a pretty purple bottle.

Here’s my case for giving it a chance—yes, pun intended. The juicy raspberry opening is playful without veering into candy territory, while the other notes give it that effortlessly polished Chanel feel. It’s the kind of fruity floral that doesn’t need to be retired the second sweater weather arrives. I say we let this one live its best life all 12 months of the year.

Of course, Chanel prices are Chanel prices (even the smallest bottle is $115), so I’d recommend giving it a test spray before committing to a full bottle—especially if you typically gravitate toward super-sweet gourmands or bold, spicy fragrances. But that being said, I’ve been wearing it regularly for well over a year and I’m nowhere near the end of my bottle, so it’ll last you a while. For anyone who loves a fresh, feminine scent with a little personality, Eau Splendide is definitely worth a sniff. That said, since it’s such an easy-to-wear, clean-meets-powdery-meets-sweet scent, I’d consider it a pretty safe blind buy if that sounds like the type of perfume you generally gravitate toward.

I’ve worn this to weddings, concerts, dinners... You name it, you can wear this perfume there. She’s effortlessly versatile, isn’t overpowering, and isn’t a snob—even though she’s polished, there’s no intimidation factor, and she doesn’t take herself too seriously. She’s a must-have for anyone, whether you’re newly dabbling in fragrance or someone who already swears by a classic fruity floral. Consider this review my formal petition to add it to a list of all-time best year-round scents.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmo who covers all things beauty, including fragrance. For this story, she shared her experience wearing the Chanel Chance Eau Splendide Eau de Parfum, one of her go-to perfumes for year-round freshness.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan and was previously the beauty assistant at Good Housekeeping, Woman's Day, and Prevention.

DWTS’ Val Chmerkovskiy Makes Spicy Comment About Jenna Johnson's Exit

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Dancing With the Stars pro Val Chmerkovskiy expressed his support for wife Jenna Johnson following their back-to-back eliminations by giving an NSFW shoutout to his “baddie” wife.

Val Chmerkovskiy and Jenna Johnson may not be adding another Mirrorball to their household, but they're still having a ball at home.

A day after Johnson and her partner Harry Shum Jr. were voted off Dancing With the Stars following his and his partner Jenna Dewan’s elimination the week prior, Chmerkovskiy showed his support for his wife with a steamy comment.

“Keep the trophy’s [sic],” the 40-year-old shared on his Instagram Stories Nov. 18 alongside a photo of Johnson from a red carpet event. “I get to watch this baddie brush her teeth naked every morning.” Chmerkovskiy—who shares son Rome, 3, with his wife of seven years—also thirsted after her by adding a drool emoji.

Johnson and Shum were shockingly sent home on the Nov. 17 semifinals after receiving perfect scores 30 out of 30 for both of their dances: a paso doble to Rihanna's "We Found Love” and a quickstep to Amy Winehouse's “Valerie,” in which Shum paid tribute to late Glee costar Naya Rivera.

While some believe viewers made a major misstep by sending the pair home, the duo slammed the negativity they said they received online, sharing they think it impacted their relationship with the rest of the season 35 cast.

“I hope to get to know them more,” Shum told E! News in an interview following their elimination Nov. 17, “because it's just been so chaotic.”

“We're a bit all trauma bonded,” Johnson chimed in, to which Shum added, “There's a lot of trauma bond just because this year has been a little crazy.”

“Social media is a little nuts,” he continued. “I hope they address that at a certain point, because I think there's a good amount of bullying that doesn't feel really good, and I think the rest of the cast feels it.”

Though Shum didn’t go into specifics, he and Johnson were spun into drama after Chmerkovskiy received a one-week suspension from the show following his now-infamous reaction to a critique judge Carrie Ann Inaba made after one of Johnson and Shum’s performances, in which he referenced Will Smith’s 2022 Oscar slap.

Shum seemingly referred to the fallout as he praised Johnson after they were voted off.

“I want to say you have been dealing with everything with grace, with the noise outside,” the Grey’s Anatomy star told his partner on air, “and teaching me the things that I have only dreamed of to learn, and this has been one of the most beautiful experiences. So, thank you so much.”

As DWTS prepares to tango into its season 35 finale, read on for a deep dive into the secrets of the ballroom…

How Much Do the Celebrities Get Paid to Be on Dancing With the Stars?

Citing multiple sources, Variety reported in 2019 that Dancing With the Stars contestants make $125,000 for the rehearsal period and first two weeks of the show. If they progress beyond that point, the outlet continued, they earn more money each week. At the time, sources told the publication stars could earn a maximum of $295,000.

However, Bobby Bones said he made more than this when he won season 27 with Sharna Burgess in 2018.

"That show pays OK," the radio personality said on a Sept. 2025 episode of Jason Tartick's podcast Trading Secrets. "Like, first episode, no money. Second episode, $10,000. I think it's like, $10,000, $10,000, $20,000, $20,000. It ends up being $50,000 an episode if you last."

Also receiving a base salary of around $110,000, Bones continued, "I ended up making close to $400,000 from that show."

ABC has not publicly confirmed any of these figures.

What Do the Pros Get Paid on Dancing With the Stars?

Similarly, little has been shared publicly about how much the pros get paid. But as with the contestants, it seems like the longer they're on the show, the more money they can make.

But even if a pro is eliminated in the first round, they're not leaving the ballroom empty-handed.

"You're guaranteed until a certain amount of weeks," Jenna Johnson said on a June 2025 episode of Kelly Stafford and Hank Winchester's podcast The Morning After. "I think there's different contracts though. I can't speak for everybody."

Do Pros Get Paid More If They Win Dancing With the Stars?

While Johnson—who won season 26 with Olympic figure skater Adam Rippon, as well as season 33 with The Bachelor's Joey Graziadei—says the pros don't get a larger paycheck if they take home what is now called the Len Goodman Mirrorball Trophy, she noted there's still an incentive to make it to the finale (besides bragging rights, of course).

"If you make it all the way to the end, you're getting paid the whole season, which is amazing, and you get a bonus on top of that for making it to the finale," she explained on The Morning After. "If you win, it's not like you win a $1 million and split it with your partner. You're just getting a cute trophy together."

Do the Troupe Members Get Paid as Much as the Pros on Dancing With the Stars?

On a 2022 episode of Trading Secrets, Lindsay Arnold recalled how her salary was cut "more than in half" when she was demoted from pro to troupe member.

How Are Pros and Celebrities Paired Together on Dancing With the Stars?

The pros get little input when it comes to being matched with a celebrity.

"You get no say," Lindsay said on a May 2025 episode of Maggie Sellers' Hot Smart Rich podcast. "It's very much just, 'Here's your partner. Make it work.'"

In fact, Jenna said the pairing is often a secret until the last minute.

"They really want to keep it a secret until you meet them live," Jenna said on The Morning After. "They want that genuine reaction. So I think people always think we know who we have and we're keeping it a secret. Absolutely not. They do not tell us. They really don't even want us to know the cast. It gets leaked a lot, but they want it very hush-hush until you walk in and meet your partner."

As for what the Dancing With the Stars team looks for when making these matches?

"It's based on height, build and personality and compatibility," former showrunner Rob Wade told E! News in 2015. "We don't pair people who aren't going to get on. It's too intense. It's not like The Bachelor or something, we couldn't do that. That would just be miserable experience for the celebrity, for us and the viewer. You don't want to see two people who don't like each other and, quite frankly, we have made that mistake pairing people up who didn't get on so well."

How Often Do the Pros and Celebrities Practice on Dancing With the Stars?

Put simply, a lot."Every day we have four-hour rehearsals," Rylee Arnold, who was partnered with Olympic gymnast Stephen Nedoroscik on season 33, shared on a September 2024 episode of the Lightweights Podcast With Joe Vulpis. "It’s either 9:00 a.m. to 1:00 p.m., 1:00 p.m. to 5:00 p.m. or 5:00 p.m to 9:00 p.m."But the work doesn't stop once the rehearsal wraps. Rylee noted she might then meet with producers or the creative team, work on choreography or study dance videos."Literally my whole life is devoted to it," she continued, "but it's my passion and it's what I love. So, it makes me so happy."Indeed, it's a no-days-off kind of gig."We have our show day on Tuesday and then it's Wednesday to Sunday, straight rehearsals," Rylee added. "And then Monday we have camera blocking and then Tuesday's show day again."

Do the Pros Get to Pick the Songs Each Week for Their Dance With Their Celebrity Partner?

"Mostly yes," Emma Slater and Britt Stewart revealed in a September 2025 Instagram video, "though it's a collaboration with producers."

What's the Process Like for Making the Costumes for Dancing With the Stars?

If you thought the quickstep was fast, just wait until you hear about the pace of the costume department."We meet with the set and lighting designers, dancers and talent and create a story [for each pair]," costume designer Daniela Gschwendtner told TV Insider back in 2017. "Then we sketch out ideas. We have five days, max, to make all the outfits. That’s half a day per costume, not including all the rhinestones. The fitting and trimming we do later. We have about 20 people in our department and then we have a separate tailor shop. It’s a big enterprise."In fact, costume designer Steven Norman Lee said pairs usually try on their costumes for the first time just hours before showtime. And while the department "might use a pair of pants again for the boys," he continued, everything is generally made custom each week.As Gschwendtner added, "We do reuse things for group numbers or promo shoots, but not for the competition. We don’t reuse things unless there’s a specific reason to do so. We try to keep everybody fresh and new in something different every week, so it stays interesting."

Lance Broadway, Former MLB Pitcher and ‘Agents Of S.H.I.E.L.D.’ Actor, Dies at 43

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Lance Broadway, a former Major League Baseball pitcher-turned-actor who appeared in Marvel’s “Agents of S.H.I.E.L.D.,” has died at 43.

Broadway was found dead on Nov. 17 in an apartment complex stairwell in Dallas, Texas, according to the Dallas County Medical Examiner. His cause of death is pending autopsy results.

Broadway was a pitcher who played for the Chicago White Sox and New York Mets. He made his feature film debut in 2013’s “Olympus Has Fallen” as Secret Service Agent O’Neill under President Benjamin Asher (Aaron Eckhart). Related Stories 'Big Mistakes' Season 2 Casts Randall Park, Debi Mazar, T.R. Knight, Jeff Perry Jeon Do-yeon on Oscar Contender 'Possible Love,' Korean Cinema's Commercial Pull and Her 'Greater Thirst' for Stories

His television career included appearances on “Agents of S.H.I.E.L.D.,” “Lucifer,” “Saint George,” “Kevin From Work” and “Real Husbands of Hollywood.” His most recent credit was on two episodes of USA Network’s “Colony” in 2016-17.

Broadway was cast as Commander Linden in “Teleios,” which was later renamed “Beyond the Trek,” in 2014. The science fiction thriller was released as a TV movie in 2017. He was cast as Devon in the short film “Shattered Reflections” in 2015.

Born in Bryan, Texas, Broadway was an all-district baseball player in high school and went on to pitch for Dallas Baptist University. He transferred to Texas Christian University, where he was named conference pitcher of the year before being selected 15th overall by the Chicago White Sox in the 2005 Major League Baseball draft. He made his Major League debut in 2007 and was traded to the New York Mets in 2009. He signed a minor league deal with the Toronto Blue Jays that same year but never played for the team.

The 10 Best Songs on the 'GTA 6' Soundtrack, Ranked

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

To say there’s been a lot of hype and anticipation around Grand Theft Auto VI is like the understatement to end all understatements. Grand Theft Auto V came out more than 13 years ago, and it was pretty mind-blowing at the time, as was Grand Theft Auto IV about five and a half years before that. The series has always been praised for the freedom it gives you, and lots of that freedom has also made the series controversial, but the last three games have done a whole lot more than just feel like mindless fun.

They’ve been giant steps forward technologically while also dealing with some more dramatic things narratively. You get the best of all the worlds, because much of the gameplay is still heightened and you can keep on causing mayhem if you want to. Anyway, that’s skipping over a lot, and it’s not even touching on the music, but music has always been a big part of these games, even going back to the GTA games of the PS2/original Xbox era. And GTA 6 is no exception, having an impressive official soundtrack (34 original songs made for the game itself), and a score by Justice… and then, of course, all the licensed music. There is so much licensed music heard on the radio stations in this game, so highlighting some of the best songs from that huge soundtrack is only scratching the surface, but these are indeed some of the greatest tracks you can listen to – mostly when you're in a (probably stolen) car – while playing GTA 6.

On the nose, sure, but if you’ve played a GTA game, you're surely aware that it’s not always the most subtle series in the world. That’s especially so for the satire, and while this writer hasn’t played enough of the most recent game to compare the humor to the previous games, many of those previous games took a South Park approach to comedy, satirizing/parodying pretty much everything that could be a decent target.

So, it’s on-the-nose. And speaking of, there’s a song on the GTA 6 V-Rock FM station called “Lawyers, Guns, and Money,” and at least two of those things are very relevant to a game where you're stealing stuff and doing acts of violence. It’s a great song on its own (Warren Zevon has a deserved cult following, but probably deserves even more popularity), and it fits the mood of driving around GTA 6 remarkably well.

This one is also a bit on-the-nose, or maybe obvious, and then further, it is a somewhat cheesy song. But… GTA 6 is a love story, given you can switch between playing two characters who are in a relationship (one with its ups and downs, sure, but that relationship is a central point of the whole game). They're Jason and Lucia, rather than Jack and Diane, but “Jack & Diane” is a broad, sweeping sort of song that really works with GTA 6’s main characters and then also, a little more abstractly, the setting/style.

The game probably elevates the song here, since in some contexts, this John Mellencamp track can feel like it lays it on a bit thick in a bad way. Maybe you just have to be in a certain mood to really appreciate it or vibe with it, but the mood can quite easily be felt while playing GTA 6, and hey, if a game or movie makes a song better, and the song sounds great as part of the soundtrack, why not include it for a ranking like this one?

If not “Pink Pony Club,” then Chappell Roan’s signature song to date is probably “Hot to Go!”, even if it might not even be her best song. It’s a big one, though. And it is fun. It’s a good fit for Flash FM, which is the modern (or fairly modern, maybe like 21st-century) pop radio station for GTA 6. Something a little moodier like “Good Luck, Babe!” might not have fit the vibe quite as well.

It’s pretty wild how good The Rise and Fall of a Midwest Princess is, as an album, that “Hot to Go!” is surrounded by a handful of songs that are even hotter. Also, seeing songs this recent is pretty great, just for the GTA 6 soundtrack generally, since the game was in development so long that there might've been something of a fear of things (music included) feeling dated by the time the game eventually came out.

It’s risky taking a song that was made for another property and putting it in something new, but it can be done, as “If You Leave” and its inclusion in GTA 6 shows. Like, this one isn't just associated with the 1986 movie Pretty in Pink because that movie happened to use the song well, but the song itself was made for that movie’s soundtrack, not appearing on an Orchestral Manoeuvres in the Dark studio album first.

Confusingly, the song “Pretty in Pink” was not written for the movie, but the movie was named after that Psychedelic Furs song. Also, that movie’s soundtrack is much better than the movie (it also features tracks by the likes of New Order, Echo & the Bunnymen, and The Smiths). That’s thankfully not the case with GTA 6. It’s a great game that just so happens to also have a great soundtrack, thanks to songs like “If You Leave.”

Now for something a bit more recent, “Lost…” well, actually, it’s not so recent that it couldn’t have technically snuck onto the GTA 5 soundtrack. It’s one of the highlights off Frank Ocean’s 2012 album Channel Orange, and GTA 5 came out in 2013, so it could've potentially just snuck on. It’s also one of those cases where it might not be the artist’s very best song, but it’s up there, and it’s also one of the most appropriate for inclusion on a GTA soundtrack.

It would be awesome to blast “Pyramids,” sure, but that song’s 10 minutes long, and you're not often going to be in a car for that long, since you'll probably get distracted by something on foot or hijack another vehicle or something in that time. And there are plenty of great songs from Blonde, but that’s more of a full album-listening experience, meaning songs plucked from there and put onto a more fractured soundtrack might not hit quite the same.

This one feels a little special, since you do hear “Drive” very early on as a needle drop, like right when you're supposed to drive to a location. Again, it’s a bit on-the-nose, but it does feel like a spontaneous and cinematic moment. The drive that you do when “Drive” is playing isn't even that wild or anything, but it’s the first time you'll be driving at night, and it is quite a quietly powerful experience.

It’s the right song for that mood, and that makes it stand out a bit more, but hey, a soundtrack choice is a soundtrack choice. Oh, and the band is called The Cars, in case a song called “Drive” being in a game called “Grand Theft Auto 6” wasn’t already beating you over the head too much. Still, a great moment is a great moment, and this song is also just a flat-out great one, so all’s fair here.

Yes, yes, lots of pop and alternative/rock kinds of songs here, and those are the songs that sound best with the style and world of GTA 6, but there is a wider variety of music found here, as is the case with all the GTA games and their radio stations. The Chamber 106.6 has some heavier stuff, like metal and a bit of punk, with Metallica (admittedly about as popular as metal bands get) being well-represented.

There are three Metallica songs that can be heard on The Chamber 106.6, and “Master of Puppets” is probably the most exciting one to hear when you're driving at a very high speed, and especially so if you're driving fast and away from a bunch of cops. It makes an already vibrant and energetic game feel more so, and it’s also a hard-to-fault song in general, so it’s a great fit for GTA 6, obviously.

It’s weird to think about “Electric Feel” feeling nostalgic, since it came out around the same time as Grand Theft Auto 4, and that game is only two GTAs ago. Maybe time flies. Anyway, “Electric Feel” was popular, sure, but it also felt like it was just cool enough to be alternative, but then it was also punchy enough to not feel out of place on mainstream radio, so if you liked any kind of music about 18 or 19 years ago, there’s a good chance you dug this one.

As for other MGMT songs? Yeah, they're a good band, or duo, technically. There’s some good stuff beyond just the songs that became surprising hits.

And it has that synthy feel that makes it feel appropriate with GTA 6 and its Vice City (and beyond) setting. Sure, not as 1980s and synthy in the way that the ‘80s-set GTA: Vice City was, way back when that came out, but the whole location has clearly retained aspects of that era. As for other MGMT songs? Yeah, they're a good band, or duo, technically. There’s some good stuff beyond just the songs that became surprising hits, especially with the slightly more recent – and overall underrated – album Little Dark Age.

“This Is the Day” best and most succinctly lays out the appeal of the artist behind it. Said artist is The The, which has always been a headache of a band name, though it’s one of those bands that’s really just one guy (named Matt Johnson). Everyone else comes and goes, a bit like a karma chameleon or something. Anyway, The The isn't a household name, but “This Is the Day” is fairly well-known, partly because it was heard on the Guardians of the Galaxy Vol. 3 soundtrack, and now it’s made its way into GTA 6 as well.

It’s one of a handful of lengthy and emotionally complicated songs found on the pretty much perfect album Soul Mining, with this track in particular striking such an admirably unique tone. It sounds a bit different depending on how you're feeling when you hear it, which is always a great quality for a song to have. If you hear it on Vice City FM (which is probably the best radio station in the game) while driving when the sun’s coming up in game, it always feels great, not to mention truly cinematic.

“Just Like Heaven” comes from what might be The Cure’s most sprawling – and perhaps messiest – album, Kiss Me, Kiss Me, Kiss Me. “Messy” isn't bad, though; it’s more just that it’s eclectic to the point of being a little inconsistent, which is something that’s harder to say about some of the band’s other albums from the 1980s. But “Just Like Heaven” is a highlight, and so it making the cut for the GTA 6 soundtrack is cool (if you could only pick one Kiss Me, Kiss Me, Kiss Me track).

That album definitely has value alone for housing this song. “Just Like Heaven” does sound like that, being a truly euphoric pop song, maybe a little alternative, but not as alternative as most songs by The Cure. They went broad here, and they did it well (the same can be said for songs that aren’t found on any GTA 6 radio station, like “Boys Don’t Cry,” “Lovesong,” and “In Between Days”).

Does Knowing Animals Are Smart and Sentient Reduce Harm?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 19, 2026 | Reviewed by Jessica Schrader

People who practice animal law depend on scholars and advocates in other fields to provide the information they need to do their important work, but does this advice really reduce or end exploitation? In their fascinating and important new book Empirical Animal Law: Testing the Assumptions of a Movement, Justin Marceau, David A. Dana, and Janice Nadler offer answers to this and other important questions by insisting that lawyers working in this field must empirically test their assumptions and learn from their mistakes.

This book should be read not only by lawyers and legal scholars but also by researchers like me. It's heartening and extremely important to see actual data about the effectiveness of research in the field of cognitive ethology, the study of animal minds, and whether or not what we learn makes a difference in the lives of the animals themselves. I remain optimistic that their detailed analyses will be a positive move for increasing animal well-being across diverse species.

Here's what Marceau had to say about this first comprehensive, data-driven account of how animal law operates in practice in which he and his co-authors call for a new empirically informed movement. This book is a gold mine of information and I couldn't agree more with what they write.

Marc Bekoff: Why did you and your colleagues write Empirical Animal Law?

Justin Marceau: It is hardly controversial to recognize that the law does a poor job of protecting the lives and interests of animals. The law allows activists who attempted to rescue beagles from cruelty to be treated as criminals, while shielding directly or through practice the very institutions that harm animals. Factory farming has increased across the U.S. in recent decades. Animals continue to be bred for our fashion, consumption, entertainment, research, and sport. And almost all of this is perfectly legal.

Much of this harm remains invisible to the public despite decades of work by activists and lawyers. For some in the movement, this means that all hope is lost. There are people out there claiming that animal protection efforts have failed. Our book is a bit more optimistic, or at least focused on what is working and what is not working.

Specifically, we are legal scholars, including Janice Nadler, a renowned social scientist, and we wanted to study the strategies and assumptions of animal advocates. What kind of assumptions are we making about how best to make progress for animals, and what could be done better? In short, we wrote the book to test the assumptions of our movement about what is working and what does not work.

JM: It is an academic book so we expect it to be useful to researchers. But we also wrote the book in an accessible format so that it can be picked up and discussed by animal advocates and lawyers across the country. Our critiques and guidance are borne out of the more than a dozen original empirical studies we conducted for the book, and we hope that the data we share in the book will help inform the next generation of advocacy on behalf of animals.

MB: What are some of the topics you consider and what are some of your major messages?

JM: The book has several different types of studies and analysis: (1) studies on the role of incremental reform in achieving more radical reform or animal rights; (2) factors that influence our opinions about the moral standing of animals; (3) the impacts for animals and humans of a carceral or tough-on-crime approach to animal law; (4) the role of protests in social change; (5) the theory of “winning by losing” in litigation or lawmaking; and (6) questions of who are the best messengers and what are the best messages for animal advocacy and outreach.

We think that each of these topics and studies brings new insights into questions that the animal protection field has either ignored or taken for granted. The major theme of the book, captured by the subtitle, is: Testing the Assumptions of a Movement.

One set of studies that we think are particularly important examines the relationship between an animal’s perceived moral standing and their Intelligence, emotional intelligence, or sentience.

Animal lawyers and advocates have long assumed that the law will "follow the science.” That is to say, the law will inevitably provide more protection as we learn more about the intelligence or capabilities of animals. "Just show them more science!" it is often proclaimed.

Our studies, drawing on thousands of participants, suggest that learning more about the inner lives of animals probably does matter, but it is complicated.

Intelligence, for example, does raise moral concern for animals, but people may disregard such findings for animals they already eat, though not always, as our results for fish show. Thus, in our studies, describing pigs as highly intelligent did not significantly raise their moral standing.

Information demonstrating that an animal can feel pain, on the other hand, increased the perceived moral standing for every species we tested, including pigs and cows. And that is a pretty remarkable finding. Informing people about scientific evidence of sentience can significantly increase the animal’s perceived moral standing.

Perhaps the most intriguing finding in this section is that learning about an animal's emotional life (that the animal is loving and cooperative, and mourns its dead) may be the most powerful factor of all. In our studies, learning about the emotional lives of animals increased moral concern even for cows, an animal most people regularly eat.

The idea that animal emotions or emotional intelligence can impact or challenge our entrenched habits of consumption or compassion for animals is fascinating to us. So far as we know, we are the first to empirically test this idea and we think it deserves considerably more study.

MB: How does your work differ from others that are concerned with some of the same general topics?

JM: To our knowledge, this is the first comprehensive effort to take up the key assumptions of animal advocates and subject them to empirical scrutiny. We can’t wait to hear what folks think about what we have found!

MB: Are you hopeful that, as people learn which kinds of information about animals’ minds, emotions, and sentience actually shift attitudes, researchers will do more to make their work accessible to a wider audience?

JM: This is key. Our research suggests that learning about the inner lives of animals can make a difference in how people perceive animals. This means that animal protection efforts need to do more to understand the science about animal lives and to translate it into forms that are digestible to the public at large.

In conversation with Justin Marceau, a most eclectic Professor of Law, Brooks Institute Faculty Research Scholar of Animal Law and Policy, and the Faculty Director of the Animal Law Program at the University of Denver University Sturm College of Law.

Jane Goodall and Donald Griffin Helped Us Get Over Ourselves.

Anne Hathaway’s 'Jurassic Park' Replacement Officially Dominates Streaming

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Anne Hathaway has had a brilliant run this year. From playing Penelope in Christopher Nolan’s The Odyssey, reprising Andy Sachs in The Devil Wears Prada 2, playing a troubled pop star in Mother Mary, to the titular character in Michael Showalter’s Verity. Hathaway’s range is on full display for her fans. However, one of her performances stayed underrated, and it’s in a Jurassic Park replacement film!

The Princess Diaries’ actor isn’t new to the sci-fi genre, having carried the emotional core of Nolan’s Interstellar and black comedy Colossal, and she proves her mettle again in this year’s The End of Oak Street, where she stars alongside Ewan McGregor. Written and directed by David Robert Mitchell, the film explores themes of working together and rediscovering a lost bond. It criticizes the human tendency to prioritize personal comfort and normalize surrounding horrors rather than face harsh truths, while exploring themes of time, evolution, and reinventing oneself.

Set in July 1982, we follow the Platt family — Denise (Hathaway), Greg (McGregor), and their children Audrey and Brian — living in a suburban neighborhood. The family is on the brink of dissolution due to marital strain and financial struggles. However, things change when a mysterious cosmic event involving wormholes rips their entire neighborhood from suburbia and transports it 200 million years into the past. The residents find themselves trapped in a hostile prehistoric landscape overrun by terrifying, aggressive dinosaurs. The Platts then fight to survive and find a portal back home, put aside their differences, and rediscover their bond.

After its theatrical run, the feature released on PVOD and streaming platforms and received its due attention. Currently, The End of Oak Street is the top movie on HBO Max’s global top 10 list. It ranks above titles like Matilda, The Legend of Tarzan, and The Hunger Games franchise. The sci-fi actioner was praised as a suspenseful, frightening, and pulpy creature feature for its practical and CGI visual effects, and Hathaway's strong performance. It grossed $121.6 million worldwide. Critics gave it an 86% Rotten Tomatoes rating, though audiences were stricter with their 76% rating. Check out the feature for Hathaway and McGregor’s compelling performances, a thrilling story, and a brilliant third act. The movie also stars Christian Convery, Maisy Stella, Jordan Alexa Davis, P.J. Byrne, Chris Coy, and more.

The End of Oak Street is streaming on HBO Max. Stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

“I’m in Preparation for the End of Our World”: Golshifteh Farahani on Art, War and Her Tallinn Premiere

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After a thriller set as Kabul braces for the Taliban, the actress says her mind is on the state of the world and not on her next role.

Golshifteh Farahani (Alpha, Pirates of the Caribbean: Dead Men Tell No Tales) is Leila, a young widow seeking a fresh start in Kabul, Afghanistan, in writer-director Chabname Zariab’s debut feature When She Hears the Bells (Les Clochettes de Kaboul).

The France-based Iranian actress is attending the 30th edition of Estonia’s Tallinn Black Nights Film Festival (PÖFF), where the film had its world premiere on Wednesday night in the First Feature Competition. On Thursday, she talked to The Hollywood Reporter about the film and its call to stand up for human rights. Related Stories Movies "Wicker Nipples" and All: 'Wicker' Cinematographer Celebrates Steamy Film's Viral Success Movies Indigenous Queer Drama 'Princess' Is Dedicated to "Everyone Fighting for Their Freedom, Their Identity"

After all, Leila soon finds out that the kitchen she works in is a so-called bacha bazi house. These are establishments in which boys and young men are forced to dress in women’s clothes and dance for entertainment — regularly followed by sexual exploitation. Set in 2021, the film also sees the Taliban moving ever closer to Kabul. Leila tries to flee while helping a group of boys escape before the city falls. Sayed Hashimi (13 Days, 13 Nights) co-stars in the thriller, which includes images actually shot in Afghanistan. Le Pacte is handling sales.

Farahani talked to THR in Tallinn about When She Hears the Bells, why the film can speak to the Western world and why she isn’t spending time thinking about her next project.

How did you find out about this film and decide to star in it?

Chabname wanted me for some time, but I wasn’t reading the script because I was thinking I’d done The Patience Stone [set in war-torn Afghanistan]. So, it’s a space for other actresses to do that. But she insisted and insisted and insisted, and at the end she said, “We’re not going to do the movie [without you].” So, I ended up accepting the part without reading the script. I called her and said, “Let’s do it.”

I couldn’t believe that this was Chabname’s first feature script and her directorial debut.

When I read the script, I was also really surprised. It was amazing. The script is as good as the movie.

When She Hears the Bells combines thriller elements with the humane and emotional elements. How hard is that combination for you as the star?

I’m happy that I could see that it works because I’m very objective when I watch my movies. I know when it’s bad and it doesn’t work, and I know when it’s good. Last night [during the premiere], I could feel this works because I constantly had goosebumps over goosebumps over goosebumps. I felt, “Yeah, the emotion works.” For one of my friends, it is impossible to watch a movie without checking his phone. It was the first time I watched him not touching his phone, and at one point he just [leaned] forward. He was really engaged, and that was the best sign. The movie works. It really works.

There are all sorts of dark issues and threats in When She Hears the Bells. There is the bacha bazi, the situation of women, the encroaching Taliban. Do you see these as sharing any common themes?

The way I always look at it is that you’re telling a story. It’s just that the story is about horrible things. I think about the character who is actually in that situation, which is terrible. From the outside, when you watch it, it is unbearable. But the character is in it, so, as the character, I don’t judge it as horrible. It’s just that I’m living in that horror.

As an audience, you say, “This is awful, this is horrible!” But as a character inside, you cannot say this is horrible. You just act on it.

Do you have any hopes for what audiences will take away from the film?

If the audience is moved by it and asks questions, if the movie is seen and appreciated, it’s enough because so many of these movies can be so boring and so much.

These subjects are too much for the white man to even comprehend. Because sometimes the horror is too much for the civilized world. They can’t even handle it. So they zap [it out] subconsciously.

But I think this movie works because it doesn’t show the actual horror. If you saw the horror [committed against] a little boy in the film, the audience would zap. But because it’s all left to the imagination, it keeps the white audience on a fine line of sitting and watching and feeling it, not blocking out the feeling.

This is the third year in a row that you have a film here at Tallinn. How much do you like the city and the festival?I love Tallinn, and I think the festival is great. I think people come here to watch movies, and the fact that this festival exists is a blessing because we all know the situation of the world and the budgets, and there are always challenges for these little festivals. But I come back to Tallinn anytime they ask me because I think it’s a great festival. I have had three movies here: Reading Lolita in Tehran, Oh, What Happy Days!, and this one.

Is there anything you can share about any potential next projects?

I’m a bit pessimistic, and I feel there’s a big war coming. So, in my head, I feel we just need to prepare for a war. My headspace is not much in the artistic world, because I feel the situation of the world is so bad, and I feel that humanity needs to be prepared for war. Especially in Europe, people have been so spoiled, and I think the first place that is going to be completely smashed is Europe. So, in my headspace, I’m in preparation for the end of the world, the end of our world. I think we’re going to enter a new order.

Who would start the war, or where?I just feel the world is going to go upside down. The world has become a minefield. No matter where you go, something is going to explode everywhere — every region you think of: America, Los Angeles, Florida, Africa, Europe, China, Taiwan. Everywhere you go, you might step on a mine, and it might explode. You just need to prepare.

Bears work to get a stadium deal in Illinois by December 3

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Bears continue to work toward getting a new stadium deal in place. And, once again, the clock is ticking.

Via Ian Rapoport of NFL Network, Bears chairman George McCaskey and Bears president and CEO Kevin Warren met with the league’s stadium committee on Wednesday to provide updates on potential sites in Illinois and Indiana.

Indiana is ready to go. The fact that the Bears have yet to go strongly suggests that they prefer Illinois.

And while prior reports indicated that a deal is expected with the Illinois legislature, the current session ends on December 3. If a deal isn’t reached by then, the can gets kicked again.

Which, in turn, raises the question of whether the Bears will finalize an agreement with Indiana.

Nothing is binding until contracts are signed. Even then, it won’t be real until construction begins.

The ultimate goal is to begin construction. At the league meetings in March, Warren told PFT Live that, with each year that the project is delayed, the expected cost increases by another $150 million.

In typical Em fashion, he put his own creative spin on the visual, drawing inspiration from his animated The Slim Shady Show from 2000.

Frank Reich: I'm the Jets' offensive coordinator, our inability to score starts with me

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Jets haven’t scored a touchdown since the third quarter of their Week 8 loss to the Raiders. It’s now Week 11, and Jets offensive coordinator Frank Reich knows it’s past time for him to find a way to get his team into the end zone.

Reich said today that he believes the Jets have enough talent on offense to produce, and he and the coaching staff need to make it happen.

“I’m the offensive coordinator, it starts with me,” Reich said. “I, and we as a staff, have to put the players in position to do what they do. We’ve got a very talented group, so we need to do a better job coaching.”

Over the last nine quarters of play, the Jets have nine Jason Sanders field goals, and no other points. Reich is well aware that three points a quarter isn’t going to win many games.

“Obviously, players need to do their part and execute and make plays,” Reich said. “For all of us, it’s a collective effort. We know we need to put more points on the board, whether it’s in the red zone or whether it’s further out, we still want to be explosive. We need to get back on track there. It’s been nine quarters. Believe me, I feel every play of that nine quarters.”

12 Best Acting Performances In Steven Spielberg Movies, Ranked

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Steven Spielberg is one of, if not the most iconic directors working today. The filmmaker’s first project dates all the way back to 1971 with the release of Duel on ABC (a direct to television film), and he has since gone on to helm countless other titles. In total, Spielberg has directed a whopping 36 projects, and is still active to this day, with the release of Disclosure Day coming earlier this year.

Many of the classic films from the last forty years can be attributed to Spielberg. The director helmed 1993s Jurassic Park, for example, and he also did the first four Indiana Jones films, along with Schindler’s List, Saving Private Ryan, Jaws, and many, many more. Impressively, Spielberg has switched genres frequently in his career, never staying in one area for too long (his two most recent projects, for example, are The Fablemans and Disclosure Day, and both are vastly different).

Spielberg isn’t just known for his pure directing talents, however. He’s also made a reputation for himself as one of the best “actor’s directors” working today; that is, having the ability to work with all kinds of unique performers and get the best out of them each and every single time.

There’s plenty of evidence to back up that claim, too. Just as some of the best movies of the last forty years have come from Spielberg, some of the best performances from actors have come in these projects as well. And while the actors naturally deserve great credit for these turns, Spielberg should also get his plaudits for being the mastermind behind these performances.

One of Spielberg’s earlier films was the iconic Jaws. Everyone knows the story of this one — a giant shark circles the waters of a coastal town, with a small group of townsfolk warning others of the dangers, but are up against a mayor who is unwilling to shut down the beaches.

Interestingly, Spielberg reportedly assembled a small group of A-list actors that he wanted to appear in the film. At the same time, the director was also hesitant to choose anyone too well known, as he didn’t want their fame to overshadow the movie. As such, the likes of Robert Redford, Paul Newman, and Steve McQueen, all names Spielberg had in mind, were not in Jaws.

One name that was in Jaws, however, is Robert Shaw. The English actor was well known at the time, both in the United Kingdom and the United States (although he was especially famous in the former, his homeland). Before Jaws, Shaw had appeared in the likes of From Russia With Love, The Taking of Pelham One Two Three, and A Man for All Seasons (for which he received an Academy Award nomination).

In Jaws, Shaw plays Quint, a grizzled and hardened former soldier who survived a massive shipwreck during World War II. He, very famously, has a strong hatred for sharks, but along with that, he also carries extensive knowledge of these animals as well. Shaw himself is arguably the standout performer of the film, especially because of his speech on the USS Indianapolis, in which he says that sharks have “lifeless eyes, black eyes, like a doll’s eyes.”

Another standout performer from Jaws is the film’s lead hero, Roy Schneider, who plays Chief Martin Brody. Like Shaw, Jaws ended up being the biggest role of Schneider’s career (and one he would reprise in future sequels, including Jaws II).

Ironically, Chief Brody has a deep-rooted fear of the water, which makes his quest for the shark even more intense. The audience can truly feel Brody’s anxiety and stress through Schneider’s performance, which, in turn, makes the movie feel more lived in and real. At the same time, Schneider does brilliantly to ensure his character isn’t defined by fear, making this one of the very best performances in any Steven Spielberg movie.

Just four years after Jaws, Steven Spielberg launched what ended up being another one of his most classic projects. Spielberg developed the character of Indiana Jones alongside George Lucas and Lawrence Kasdan, and then helmed Jones’ first movie, Raiders of the Lost Ark.

Harrison Ford’s first performance as Indiana Jones remains just as iconic as it was nearly fifty years ago. Ford exudes confidence and swagger, making Indiana Jones one of the very best heroes in movie history. After the first Star Wars movie in 1977, it would have been hard to predict that Ford would come up with another character just as iconic, if not more so than Han Solo, but he does just that in Raiders of the Lost Ark (thanks, in part, to Steven Spielberg’s stellar direction).

As far as intimate projects go, arguably no film in recent history has been as meaningful to its director as The Fablemans. The 2022 film serves as Spielberg’s very own origin story, showing his upbringing and how he became interested in filmmaking in the first place. His parents play big roles in the film, especially his mother, who’s played by Michelle Williams, in what is one of the finest performances of her entire career.

Williams has given many memorable turns over the years, but The Fablemans truly stands out. Williams is truly outstanding in this film, playing a character who is so conflicted (and someone that Spielberg himself clearly has complicated feelings about). It’s far from the easiest role to play, especially when factoring in that this is her director’s mother, but Williams deserves great credit.

Arguably the main attraction of Jurassic Park as a film are the dinosaurs. The special effects were exceptional in 1993 and remain so to this day, and along with Spielberg’s direction, help to make these prehistoric creatures feel truly alive. Yet, at the same time, the human characters are just as impressive in Jurassic Park, particularly Jeff Goldblum’s Dr. Ian Malcom.

Malcom is a mathematician who excels in chaos theory, and is highly skeptical about John Hammond’s Earth-shattering invention right from the get-go. Goldblum plays Malcom tremendously, making him a witty character that, at the same time, also demands to be taken seriously. Goldblum helps tie the entire movie together, in fact, making this another top-tier performance in a Steven Spielberg film.

Spielberg incredibly made not one, but two incredible films in 1993. One was the aforementioned Jurassic Park, while the other was a vastly different story — Schindler’s List. The film told the true story of a German industrialist named Oskar Schindler who put his life on the line to save thousands of Jewish people from the Holocaust.

Liam Neeson plays Schindler in what is likely the finest performance of his entire career. There’s so much emotion entrenched in Schindler’s List, and that doesn’t just come from the script or Spielberg’s direction (both of which are excellent). Neeson is extraordinary here, proving himself more than capable of delivering an iconic dramatic performance.

Another iconic 1990s film from Spielberg is Saving Private Ryan, which focuses on a mission to save Private James Francis Ryan (Matt Damon) after his three brothers were killed during the Battle of Normandy. Ryan is the core subject of the film, but Tom Hanks’ Captain John Miller is the one leading the mission.

Hanks is often labeled as one of the best actors to ever live, and Saving Private Ryan is often cited as evidence for this. It’s for good reason, too, as Hanks is this film’s standout. Despite Hanks’ immense fame, it’s easy to forget that he’s there at all, and instead, audiences are likely to simply see Captain Miller, which makes Saving Private Ryan feel all the more realistic.

Arguably the most divisive entry on this list, Tom Cruise received mixed responses to his performance as the leading protagonist in Spielberg’s War of the Worlds remake. The film itself received mixed reception, too, but Cruise does deserve a lot of credit for his performance here. Cruise plays a single dad incredibly well, and embodies what it feels like to be a parent during moments of danger.

One of the very best movies that Steven Spielberg ever made also features Tom Hanks - Catch Me if You Can. The film, which centers on Leonardo DiCaprio’s Frank Abagnale Jr., is actually a true story that you have to see to believe. DiCaprio’s Abagnale Jr. is eccentric, coming up with lies on the spot. As a result, he lives several different lives throughout the film, all with the FBI hot on his trail.

Hanks is, predictably, excellent as the FBI agent leading the charge. Yet, he’s outshone by DiCaprio, only in his early 20s at this time. And despite the fact that this was relatively early on in DiCaprio’s career, it remains one of his very best, and one of the very best performances in any Steven Spielberg film.

Whoopi Goldberg had her breakout role in Steven Spielberg’s 1985 adaption of The Color Purple. It’s no wonder that Goldberg became a star after, too, as she steals the film, vastly improving the story and making it more than worth the watch. A huge reason for this is the pain that Goldberg embodies throughout, making her character feel like a truly three-dimensional and real human being.

‘Landman’ Star’s New Racing Thriller Officially Unleashes Star-Studded Trailer

Andy Garcia is among the names attached to star in Maserati: The Brothers, the new film coming to theaters on January 29, 2027. The show that claimed the most of your answers is the world you were built for. If two tied, both are shown — you're complicated enough to straddle two Sheridan universes.

You are a Dutton — or you might as well be. You understand that some things are worth protecting at any cost, and that the modern world's indifference to history, to land, to legacy, is not something you're willing to accept quietly. You lead from the front, you carry your family's weight without complaint, and when someone threatens what's yours, you don't escalate — you finish it. You're not cruel. But you are absolute. In Yellowstone's world, that combination of ferocity and loyalty doesn't make you a villain. It makes you the only thing standing between everything that matters and everyone who wants to take it.

You thrive in the chaos of high-stakes negotiation, where the money is enormous, the margins are thin, and the wrong word in the wrong room can cost everyone everything. You're a fixer — the person called when a situation is already on fire and needs someone with the nerve to walk into it. West Texas oil country rewards exactly what you are: sharp, adaptable, unsentimental, and absolutely clear-eyed about what people want and what they'll do to get it. You're not naive enough to think this world is fair. You're smart enough to be the one deciding who it's fair to.

You are a Dwight Manfredi — someone who has served their time, paid their dues, and arrived somewhere unexpected with nothing but their reputation and their wits. You adapt without losing yourself. You build loyalty through respect rather than fear, though you're not above reminding people that the two aren't mutually exclusive. Tulsa King is for people who are still standing when everyone assumed they'd be finished — who find, in an unfamiliar place, that they're more capable than the world gave them credit for. You don't need a throne. You build one, wherever you happen to land.

You carry the weight of a system that is broken by design, and you do it anyway — because someone has to, and because you're the only one positioned to do it without the whole thing collapsing. Mike McLusky's world is for people who are comfortable operating where there are no good options, only less catastrophic ones. You speak every language: law enforcement, criminal, political, human. That fluency makes you invaluable and it makes you a target. You've made your peace with both. Mayor of Kingstown belongs to people who understand that keeping the peace is not the same as being at peace — and who do the job regardless.

From host Lainey Wilson to Sugarland and beyond, country stars took part in Swift's "I Knew It, I Knew You" and "Our Song" set.

Man, it’s been awhile – 13 years to be exact – since Taylor Swift last performed at the CMA Awards, but when she returned to take the stage at the Bridgestone Arena in Nashville on Wednesday (Nov. 18), she did it in style and made sure it sparkled not only thanks to her sequined outfit, but with literal stars – country music stars, that is.

The whimsical performance – a mashup of her Toy Story 5 hit (which has topped the Billboard Hot 100 for two weeks so far) “I Knew It, I Knew You” and “Our Song” (No. 16 peak) from her self-titled 2006 debut album – kicked off backstage in her dressing room. Eagle-eyed Swifties likely noticed that quite a few of her debut-era outfits were strategically placed around the space. But look a little closer, and viewers might see that she had a very small but special – albeit not breathing – guest in the room: Jessie the doll from the Toy Story franchise.

And as Swift left her dressing room and moved through the backstage area as she effortlessly sang “I Knew It, I Knew You,” country greats such as Little Big Town, Brooks & Dunn, host Lainey Wilson, Eric Church and “Whispering Bill” Anderson all greeted her on the way, with Keith Urban waiting on the Toy Story-inspired stage to perform alongside her.

Swift kept Paisley busy, as the “Whiskey Lullaby” crooner donned some rubber gloves to polish her CMA Awards trophies.

Cheese! The pop superstar struck a pose with Little Big Town for a quick snap.

Swift plopped Kix Brooks’ burgundy cowboy hat on Lainey Wilson’s head, while the CMA Awards host handed the singer a pair of sunglasses.

Swift stopped for a quick and cool fist bump with Eric Church.

“Whispering Bill” was all smiles as he handed the superstar artist her sparkling Toy Story-themed guitar.

Urban was the only country artist to join Swift for the onstage part of her performance.

Man Of Tomorrow’s Superman Improvements Teased By David Corenswet Amid DCU Concerns

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ryan Northrup is a Senior Staff Writer on ScreenRant's Movie & TV News team, where he has authored nearly 5,000 articles, including features and reviews. A graduate of McMaster University, Ryan began his career in public relations before attending Vancouver Film School. There, he built lasting industry connections and optioned a feature film screenplay.

Expect some Superman upgrades in Man of Tomorrow.

Following his introduction as Superman in James Gunn's movie of the same name last year, David Corenswet is set to return as the DC hero next summer for an anticipated sequel. Man of Tomorrow, which arrives following a shakeup at DC in which Gunn and Peter Safran are to step down from their executive leadership roles, will see Superman teaming up with Nicholas Hoult's Lex Luthor to face a new threat.

As the future of DC remains up in the air, Corenswet dropped by The Rich Eisen Show to discuss his time as Superman and what's to come in Man of Tomorrow. Corenswet comments on what it was like filming the original movie and all the firsts that came with that experience, not just in terms of physically transforming himself, but also in terms of becoming comfortable in the suit. He explains:

I mean, looking back, there were a lot of great challenges that came along with it: gaining the weight for the first time in my life, the wire work, the stunt work was a new thing for me. That was wonderful and challenging. I think back to what Christopher Reeve said about his thoughts going into shooting the original Superman: The Movie which was… Ultimately, it’s just you out there in the tights and nobody’s coming to save you.

And so sometimes the biggest challenge was just standing there for a scene in the tights and the trunks and somehow finding a way to not feel silly and not try too hard to make up for it in some way, not act too tough or shy away from what it is. I think it’s one of the great things about the character is that he’s proud to be the big red and blue and he’ll just stand there in his trunks and maybe you’ll laugh at him, but he’ll be happy to have made you laugh.

While addressing how he maintains his confidence while acting in tights next to his costars, Corenswet touches on Lex's advanced armor suit set to feature in the Superman sequel. The actor then jokingly asks why his character doesn't get his own armored suit:

It was really nice anytime I would walk up to somebody and they’d go like, “Oh my god, it’s Superman.” I go, “Okay, I’m going to take that with me for the next few minutes and hopefully that’ll keep my confidence up just long enough to get through the next scene.” Especially when you’re standing across from Rachel Brosnahan, who’s dressed all cool, and she looks so beautiful, and she’s so sharp, and Nicholas Hoult, and his cool tailored suits, and then his war suit in this movie we just shot. He’s got his big green war suit. Like, why can’t I get some armor?

Though Corenswet is careful not to reveal too much about Man of Tomorrow, he does tease that the film features some exciting flying and fight sequences. Speaking seemingly of the stunt and visual effects teams, Corenswet says that "they really up their game" from the first movie. Check out his comment below:

Love the interview scene with Rachel. That was such a fun scene to shoot. It was the first scene that we shot. It was two days and it was like shooting like a 1950s Cary Grant movie. Just the dialogue, quippy, and funny, and just about these characters. There were so many great action pieces. Actually, in this movie, in the next Superman movie that we finished this summer, there are some incredible new flying and fights. They really up their game there. So, in some ways, those are the ones I’m really excited for people to see those, ’cause those were a lot of fun to shoot and I think will end up being a lot of fun to do.

In addition to Corenswet, Brosnahan, and Hoult, Man of Tomorrow is set to feature the return of Skyler Gisondo as Jimmy Olsen, Wendell Pierce as Perry White, Isabela Merced as Hawkgirl, Frank Grillo as Rick Flag Sr., Edi Gathegi as Mr. Terrific, Mikaela Hoover as Cat Grant, and María Gabriela de Faría as the Engineer, among others.

The Superman follow-up will also feature a host of newcomers, including Lars Eidinger as Brainiac, Aaron Pierre as Green Lantern, Milly Alcock as Supergirl, Xolo Maridueña as Blue Beetle, and Matthew Lillard in an undisclosed role. Man of Tomorrow's upgraded flying and fighting scenes, then, will clearly accompany a much more expansive and star-studded cast.

Of course, the recent leadership shakeup at DC Studios has cast a shadow over Man of Tomorrow and has raised questions about the current iteration of DC heroes. After becoming co-CEOs of DC Studios in 2022, Gunn and Safran are confirmed to be stepping down from their roles effective December 1, 2026.

The development follows Paramount Skydance's acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery on October 6, with the resulting company simply called Skydance. Jeremy Latcham is stepping in as president and chief creative officer, replacing Gunn and Safran.

Man of Tomorrow will follow a mixed series of releases for DC. Superman was a modest hit, grossing $618 million and earning strong reviews. Supergirl (2026), however, was a box office bomb this summer. Most recently, Clayface, which hit theaters on October 23, earned positive reviews and became a modest box office success. Gunn and Safran were also broadly successful on the TV front, with Peacemaker, Creature Commandos, and Lanterns all met with warm receptions.

Though many questions remain about Man of Tomorrow and the future of DC more broadly, audiences can evidently expect some exciting upgrades to Superman's flying and fighting scenes. With the sequel potentially serving as Corenswet's last outing as the DC hero, the movie is certainly shaping up to end things on a high note.

Christopher Nolan Explains Why Fans Will Never See an Odyssey Director’s Cut

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Don’t expect to see any deleted scenes in the epic’s home release

With a runtime of just under three hours, Christopher Nolan's The Odyssey doesn't necessarily need to be any longer, even as an adaptation of Homer's epic poem. Although it isn't Nolan's longest film, fans are always clamoring for more from the prolific director. With The Odyssey's release on home media​​​​​​​, there was some hope that a director's cut or deleted scenes would surface. Nolan has shut that idea down, going so far as to say people will likely never see director's cuts of his films.

Nolan has been pretty excited about the home release of The Odyssey. While the director is known for shooting in IMAX and encouraging audiences to see his movies on the biggest screens possible, he also knows home viewing has a place in movie culture. Speaking to Polygon, Nolan said that at home the viewer is able to control the timeline and look at details, specifically saying that "it's really about the performances." He also supports talking over the movie​​​​​​​ in the comfort of your home.

Still, home releases often come with bonuses such as deleted scenes. Nolan told The Independent, however, that isn't his style. “I don’t really believe in director’s cuts. I want to stand by the film that goes out,”​​​​​​​ the director said. He added that, in the case of The Odyssey, "there are a lot of deleted scenes up in the wastebasket that no one will ever see." Nolan stresses​​​​​​d that there aren't actually too many scenes that didn't make the final cut because shooting on film requires planning. "I tend to try and weed things out on paper because it’s crazy expensive to shoot things that aren’t going to be in the film." He admitted that this has led to a lack of bonuses on home releases, "which always disappoints the DVD crowd."

For those watching the movie for the first time or the tenth time​​​​​​​, Nolan does have some opinions on how to best emulate the movie theater experience. Specifically, he suggests people get a projector. "You can get a relatively low-cost, pretty high-quality projector, darken your living room, maybe introduce some popcorn into the equation or whatever," he told Polygon. "Have a mini movie theater and do it on your own timeline. That's tremendous fun."

Over the course of his career, Nolan said that he's been impressed by how much the home-viewing experience has improved thanks to technology like better projection technology​​​​​​​. Because of that, he told Polygon that a film like The Odyssey carries its cinematic excitement and spectacle to people's living rooms. "Even if people are seeing it for the first time, I think the film sort of carries that with it."

Stephen Colbert Signs With UTA

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Multiple Emmy Award winner Stephen Colbert and his production company Spartina Industries have signed with United Talent Agency for representation across film, TV, publishing, and more.

Colbert will continue to be managed by James ‘Baby Doll’ Dixon of DixonTalent, whom he has worked with for over 20 years.

The long-time late-night host, writer, and executive producer recently concluded his top-rated CBS series The Late Show with Stephen Colbert, which ran for 11 seasons. The series finale reached 6.74M live + same-day viewers, per preliminary panel-only data from Nielsen, becoming the most-watched weeknight episode ever, as Deadline reported in May. The series-high ratings were reached with a little help from his friends Paul McCartney, the Strike Force Five: Jimmy Kimmel, Jimmy Fallon, John Oliver and Seth Meyers, Jon Stewart, Andy Cohen, as well as Bryan Cranston, Paul Rudd, Tim Meadows, Elijah Woods and Ryan Reynolds. Related Stories News Rola Bauer Takes On Creative Director Role At Monte-Carlo Festival As Talent Agents & Producers Join Advisory Board Agencies 'Call Her Daddy's Alex Cooper Officially Moves To CAA After Leaving UTA

Prior to that, he hosted, wrote, and produced The Colbert Report on Comedy Central for 9 years, following his 7 years as a writer and correspondent on The Daily Show. He also recently played a fictional TV show host on an episode of the CBS series Elsbeth, working alongside Andy Richter and Amy Sedaris, in a special yet unofficial Strangers With Candy reunion. Watch on Deadline

Spartina Industries produced the late-night program After Midnight on CBS for two seasons and has several projects in development, including a series adaptation of Roger Zelazny’s sci-fi novels, The Chronicles of Amber, with Robert Kirkman’s Skybound Entertainment. Carrie Byalick serves as Head of Spartina Industries.

“We are thrilled to be working with Stephen Colbert. He is an exceptionally talented artist and creator, a dedicated philanthropist and a terrific person,” shared UTA President of Filmed Entertainment Matt Rice and Managing Partners Jay Gassner and Julien Thuan. “We look forward to partnering with Stephen and Spartina Industries to support and further expand his creative endeavors.”

Up next for Colbert is tackling Middle Earth. He is currently co-writing the next installment of the Lord of the Rings film franchise with Philippa Boyens and Peter McGee for New Line Cinema and Warner Bros. The Lord of the Rings: Shadow of the Past, as the project is tentatively titled, will be based on “Fogs on the Barrow-downs,” the eighth chapter of The Fellowship of the Ring, where Hobbits are trapped by a Barrow-wight in an unnatural fog, as Deadline reported in March. The movie will be derived from chapters 3 to 8 of Tolkien’s book.

Colbert will also continue to be repped by Hansen, Jacobson, Teller, Hoberman.
