Robert Pattinson Is The New James Bond

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It looks like Robert Pattinson is trading in his bat mobile for an Aston Martin. After a highly scrutinized and lengthy audition process involving some of Hollywood's most sought after leading men, it was the Batman star who emerged at the eleventh hour to nab the highly sought after role of M16 agent James Bond.

“James Bond is one of the most legendary characters in cinema, and to be entrusted with 007 is the honor of a lifetime,” said Pattinson in a statement. “I’m deeply grateful to Denis, Amy, David, Tanya and everyone at Amazon MGM Studios for their belief in me. To say this role is a dream come true is an understatement. I couldn’t be more excited about this partnership and the work ahead with this fantastic team.”

Created by Ian Fleming for his 1953 novel Casino Royale, James Bond, the British intelligence officer who lends his immense talents to M16, is one of the most recognizable characters in the modern world. Figuring prominently in twelve of Fleming’s novels and two short stories, Bond is both tall, dark, and handsome and calm, cool, and collected—the platonic ideal of a world class spy. Bond leapt from page to screen in 1962 with the Eon-produced Dr. No, spawning one of the biggest media franchises in history. Since its inception, the twenty-five Bond films have grossed over $7 billion dollars world wide.

Pattinson follows in the suave footsteps of Daniel Craig, who played James Bond for five films, from 2006's Casino Royale until 2022's No Time To Die where (spoiler alert) Craig's 007 dies while protecting the world from a global terrorist organization. Around No Time To Die, there was another shake up in Bond ownership when Amazon acquired Bond's parent studio, MGM, in an $8.5 billion. Barbara Broccoli, daughter of Eon founder Albert R. “Cubby” Broccoli, producer of the first Bond film and her half brother, Michael G. Broccoli, Barbara held the rights for the franchise for almost 30 years, and pledged to work with Amazon after founder Jeff Bezos. However, in February 2025, the Broccolis announced that they’d be stepping back from the franchise and ceding complete creative control to Amazon MGM. At the Oscars a month later, the Broccoli family was honored with a bizarre musical tribute to James Bond, starring Margaret Qualley, Doja Cat, and Lisa, unofficially ushering in a new era in the Bond Cinematic Universe.

Although a new era of Bond loomed, Amazon MGM had one major problem: they needed a new Bond. Ahead of No Time To Die, Craig had announced that he was stepping away from the franchise, leaving the producers with a 007 sized hole to fill. Before she stepped away, Broccoli insinuated that this new era of Bond was looking for a younger actor, in their 30s, to take over the reins from Craig, who was 51 when he retired from M16.

Technically, they landed on someone slightly outside their preferred age range; Pattinson is 40 years old. Just don't tell him that. During Vanity Fair's lie detector test with Jennifer Lawrence while promoting their film Die My Love, Pattinson insisted he was from a different generation. I am Gen Z," he said to Lawrence. "I am." Hey, you're only as old as you feel. In the same video, Pattinson said he “loves taking jobs of younger actors," which he managed to do with his Bond casting. Good for Pattinson.

Almost every leading man in Hollywood's name came up as a potential predecessor to Craig—except for Pattinson, who the masses assumed was out of contention due to the fact that he locked into playing another beloved fictional character with massive IP: Batman. Pattinson's take on the caped crusader in Matt Reeve's The Batman (2022) was a major success, but several production delays on The Batman Part II, and a shakeup at Batman's studio DC—last week, it was announced that co-CEO's of DC James Gunn and Peter Safran were ousted from the studio—meant that there might be time in Pattinson's schedule for some sexy espionage.

Still, it was far from a done deal that Pattinson would land 007. In recent years, some fans have advocated for a twist on the classic Bond formula. Lashawna Lynch made history as Nomi, the first Black person or woman to ever to hold the 007 moniker in No Time To Die. But Nomi is not James Bond. There was a seemingly endless period when the world was convinced that Idris Elba—arguably the most tall, dark, and handsome man to ever exist—would take on the mantle at some point, but while Elba auditioned, it wasn’t meant to be. Now, at 53, Elba has seemingly aged out of the role. (For the record, Elba thought the next Bond should be a woman.) Elba wasn’t the only name bandied about in recent years. It was reported that Aaron Taylor Johnson was close to signing a contract to take over the role in 2023, but those reports turned out to be false.

Although a favorite at the beginning, Jacob Elordi reportedly didn't screen test for the role. Riz Ahmed made an Emmy-nominated television show, Bait, inspired by the idea of a British Pakastani actor getting cast as Bond. Other actors rumored to be in the mix over the course of the process include, but are not limited to Tom Francis, Harris Dickinson and apparently Harry Styles. At the end of the day five actors screen tested for the role: Slow Horse's Jack Lowden, One Night Only's Callum Turner, Disclosure Day's Josh O'Connor, Superman's Nicholas Hoult, and Pattison. Hoult and Pattinson were the last two to screen test for the role, implying that the powers that be at Amazon MGM may not have been happy with the three options presented and sent a hail mary. When Puck reported that Pattinson was able to negotiate a 3 picture deal rather than a 4 picture deal despite the fact that he commanded a much higher salary then the other options, it looked like the writing was on the wall that Pattinson would be the next Bond.

It also helps that Pattinson is having a career high at the moment, having starred in a handful of the most critically acclaimed and commercially successful films of 2026. He began the year by turning his controversial A24 comedy The Drama with Zendaya into a box office success, generating $134 million worldwide on a budget of $28 million. He then followed that up with a deliciously sniveling turn in Christopher Nolan's epic The Odyssey, an Oscar front-runner which earned $1.8 billion at the box office. Then there's Primetime, Lance Oppenheim's To Catch A Predator drama which stars Pattinson as host Chris Hansen. (Yes, in a way Chris Hansen is now one degree from being James Bond. Let that sink in). Primetime hit big with $61 million globally on an $18 million budget, and is earning Pattinson leading actor buzz. On top of it all, he's still got Dune: Part 3 with Villeneuve coming down the pipeline in December.

Ultimately, the Twilight star was handpicked by the powers that be at Amazon MGM, producers Amy Pascal, David Heyman, executive producer Tanya Lapointe, and director Denis Villeneuve, who is helming the next iteration of the series based on a script from screenwriter Steven Knight. Given the fact that Pattinson is starring in Villeneuve's highly-anticipated Dune: Part 3 it might not be that much of a surprise that the director chose Pattinson as his next Bond. “Before anything else, I want to express my deep gratitude to the remarkable actors who joined us on this journey," said Villeneuve in a statement. "We were surrounded by extraordinary talent, but there can only be one 007. I am overjoyed to welcome Robert Pattinson as our new James Bond. He has the timeless soul of 007 and brings a spirit that is completely new. I cannot wait to begin.”

Pattinson enters a long lineage of spectacularly suave spies—some who lasted longer than others in Bond’s impeccably tailored suits. The first ever James Bond was, of course, Sean Connery, who went on to star in seven Bond films, including From Russia with Love (1963), Goldfinger (1964), and Diamonds Are Forever (1971). Connery was followed by George Lazenby, who starred in a single Bond film, On Her Majesty’s Secret Service (1969), before passing the reins back to Connery. The third Bond was Roger Moore, who took over for 1973’s Live and Let Die, and appeared in a total of seven Bond films, most notably The Spy Who Loved Me (1977) and Octopussy (1983).

After Moore came Timothy Dalton, who became 007 with 1987’s The Living Daylights, then threw in the towel after 1989’s License to Kill. Millennials will fondly remember the next Bond: Pierce Brosnan, who starred as the sexy spy in four films from 1995’s Golden Eye to 2002’s Die Another Day, until Craig took over in 2006.

Will Pattison's Bond be more debonair like Brosnan, who told VF he was “eagerly” awaiting the Bond casting news, or brooding like Craig? Will he be remembered as a rightful heir to Connery's throne, or will his Bond feel closer to Austin Powers in The Spy who Shagged Me? In any case, lets hope Pattinson prefers his martinis shaken not stirred. He's going to be drinking a lot of them for the foreseeable future.

Harrison Ford Exec-Produced Voyaging Film ‘The Navigator’ Unveils Clip Ahead of DOC NYC Premiere (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

“The Navigator,” a voyaging film directed by Jordan Manley and executive produced by Harrison Ford, has unveiled an exclusive clip ahead of its Nov. 19 East Coast bow at DOC NYC.

Ford also supplied a message with the clip.

“‘The Navigator’ is a deeply moving film – one I’m truly grateful for. Its powerful storytelling and cinematic beauty left me in awe. Master navigator Nainoa Thompson, his teachers, and his students are extraordinary examples of profound humility. They help us understand why ancient wisdom matters in a modern world, and remind us what we are capable of when we act in harmony with nature,” Ford said. Related Stories How Netflix‘s Animated 'Steps’ Reimagines the Cinderella Story Through the Eyes of Her Asian Stepsisters and Finds a New Kind of Happily Ever After ‘Virgin River’ Renewed for Season 9 to Become Netflix’s Longest-Running Original Scripted Series

Ford is one of seven executive producers on the project, a group that also includes Yvon Chouinard and Laurene Powell Jobs. Kathy Thompson and Laura Yale are the producers, and Yo-Yo Ma contributes cello to a score by Matthew Robert Cooper.

The film’s world premiere took place Oct. 21 at Hawaiʻi Theater on Oʻahu during the Hawaii International Film Festival, and it left with the Pacific Awards – Feature prize. Thompson, meanwhile, received the Halekulani Legacy Award.

In 1980, Thompson crossed from Hawai’i to Tahiti by voyaging canoe using neither a compass nor modern instruments, becoming the first Native Hawaiian to do so in six centuries.

Now nearly 50 years later, he is making the crossing again, for what the film presents as the last time. Thompson sails with a handful of student navigators. They rely on the stars, natural cues and the lessons of the late Mau Piailug, a master navigator, along a 2,400-mile route their ancestors sailed. Piailug, who was raised on Satawal in Micronesia, taught Native Hawaiians traditional navigation.

Manley said in a director’s statement: “I came to see a deep burden and ‘kuleana’ that rests upon the shoulders of Nainoa Thompson, who has spent the last 50 years of his life fulfilling a promise made to his teacher, Micronesian master navigator Mau Piailug, to prevent navigation from going extinct.”

The director, based in Vancouver, previously made “Treeline” and the series “A Skier’s Journey.” Submarine is handling sales and distribution inquiries.

Shondaland has brought on Amy Jo Rivkin in the role of vice president of production, with Karyn Smith-Forge joining the company as vice president of scripted series.

“Amy Joy’s impressive track record steering complex, premium productions and Karyn’s vast experience and sharp instincts across scripted genres make them powerful additions to Shondaland,” said Alison Eakle, Shondaland’s chief content officer. “We’re thrilled we get to shape our next chapter of storytelling alongside them.” Related Stories 'Nobody Wants This' Renewed for Season 4 at Netflix Netflix’s 'Haunted Hotel' Renewed for Season 3

Rivkin will oversee physical production for Shondaland. Prior to joining the company, she worked at Michael Ellenberg’s Media Res for four years, where she also oversaw physical production on shows like “The Morning Show” for Apple TV. Prior to that, she worked in the Netflix original series, drama, group as well as at Legendary Entertainment.

Smith-Forge will lead development of new series at Shondaland under their expansive overall deal with Netflix. She was most recently the president of Don Cheadle’s production company, This Radicle Act Productions. She was also previously senior vice president of production for Berlanti Productions.

Shondaland has been based at Netflix since 2017. It was announced over the summer that the company would remain at the streamer for a further five years. In addition to serving as the exclusive home to Shondaland film and TV projects, the overall deal also covers gaming, merchandise, and live events connected to the IP.

Shondaland’s biggest show to date on Netflix is “Bridgerton,” which aired its fourth season earlier this year and is currently prepping its fifth. It is one of the most popular show’s in Netflix history, with Seasons 1 and 3 ranking in the Top 10 Netflix shows of all time with 113 million views and 106 million views respectively. Along with the previous prequel about Queen Charlotte, Netflix recently greenlit a second “Bridgerton” prequel about a young Lady Violet.

Paul Rudd And Seann William Scott's R-Rated Comedy Is Worth Revisiting 18 Years Later

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Studios abandoned big comedies years ago. But in the 2000s and early 2010s, we really had it good. This was the age of the Judd Apatow comedy, in which the director not only delivered such modern classics as "The 40-Year-Old Virgin" and "Knocked Up," but influenced almost every comedy released during the era. Alongside the main Apatow offerings you had those which he produced such as "Anchorman: The Legend of Ron Burgundy," or those made by his collaborators, such as "Superbad." Then, there were those films that featured Apatow associates but were made without his direct involvement. The brilliant "I Love You, Man" is one example which, despite being criminally overlooked, remains one of Paul Rudd's best films.

As does 2008's "Role Models," which saw Rudd team up with Seann William Scott for a film that, like pretty much every comedy movie released at the time, was defined by the improvisational, R-rated humor that had made Apatow's films so successful. This particular movie, however, paired Apatow allies Rudd and Christopher Mintz-Plasse with former members of sketch comedy troupe The State in director David Wain and writer/actor Ken Marino.

As someone who watched "Role Models" when it came out, I simply cannot believe this movie is 18 years old. Despite being less well known than the aforementioned "Knocked Up" and its ilk, "Role Models" became instantly beloved by me and my friends upon its release. Almost two decades later, it's strange to think that an entire generation has grown up without films such as this shaping their sense of humor. If you happen to be part of that group, now's the time to acquaint yourself with this  oft-overlooked comedy. If, like me, you remember "Role Models" fondly, you'll be happy to hear it holds up.

Like so many of the best comedies of the 2000s, "Role Models" succeeds because despite being an R-rated comedy with frequent gross-out gags and unapologetically immature humor, it's also a surprisingly tender story with its heart in the right place.

Paul Rudd plays Danny who, alongside Seann William Scott's Anson Wheeler, works as a promoter for the energy drink "Minotaur." Danny feels deeply unfulfilled and has become deeply cynical as a result, spending most of his time correcting people on their speech. This causes his girlfriend, Beth (Elizabeth Banks), to break up with him, after which Danny loses it during a presentation at a high school. His freak out leads to both he and Anson being assigned to community service at Sturdy Wings, a big brother-style program where the pair become mentors to two "littles."

Anson is paired with Ronnie (Bobb'e J. Thompson) a foul-mouthed kid with no friends, while Danny is given Augie Farks (Christopher Mintz-Plasse), a teenager obsessed with his medieval LARP group. At first, Danny and Anson are merely going through the motions. But over time they develop a bond with their "littles" — one which just might melt Danny's cold, cynical heart.

The secret star of "Role Models" is Joe Lo Truglio, who plays Kuzzik, a particularly dedicated LARP-er who, if you watch the bloopers, made it almost impossible for Rudd to get through any of his scenes without breaking. But there are a ton of standout performances here, from A.D. Miles as overly earnest Sturdy Wings counsellor Martin Gary to Ken Jeong as the self-serious LARP king Argotron and Jane Lynch as a former addict turned Sturdy Wings leader.

"Role Models" hit theaters in November 2008 and grossed a solid $92.6 million on a $28 million budget. It also garnered positive reviews, earning a 77% "certified fresh" rating on Rotten Tomatoes. Dana Stevens of Slate found "Role Models" to be "strewn with enough serendipities to make it a worthy variation on the dudes-grow-up genre," while the San Francisco Chronicle's Peter Hartlaub noted how, while Seann William Scott and Paul Rudd were "from two different moviemaking cliques" they ultimately "get along fine in a Bing Crosby/David Bowie kind of way."

Meanwhile, Rafer Guzman of Newsday noted how Rudd, who co-wrote the script with David Wain, Timothy Dowling, and Ken Marino, had "clearly learned a few things working on Judd Apatow comedies." By that point, Rudd had appeared in Apatow's "The 40-Year-Old Virgin" and "Knocked Up," as well as several Apatow-produced projects including "Anchorman" and "Forgetting Sarah Marshall." As such, he was well-established as one of the most prominent figures of the 2000s R-rated comedy boom.

But The State boys certainly brought their own flair to the film and the pairing of theirs and Rudd's sensibility along with Scott's lovable knucklehead charm made for a hilarious and surprisingly heartwarming experience that deserves to be remembered as fondly as some of the more well-known comedies of the era. If you fancy giving one of the most underrated buddy comedies of the 2000s another go, or are yet to see the "Role Models," the movie is available to watch for free on Pluto TV.

Sean McVay: Aaron Donald never ceases to amaze me, he's one-of-one

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For the first time in 1,050 days, Rams defensive tackle Aaron Donald recorded a sack when he brought down rookie Carson Beck during the second quarter of Sunday’s victory over the Cardinals.

It was the first of two takedowns he was involved in on the day, as he was also credited for a half-sack later in the afternoon.

After the contest, head coach Sean McVay told reporters Donald “never ceases to amaze me.”

“I’m never surprised,” McVay said in his press conference. “I think the term ‘one-of-one’ is thrown around way too loosely in this day and age, but it’s not with him. He’s one-of-one. He can do things, he’s done things and his impact — whether he has to say it or just show it, he’s a total ‘G.’ I love everything he’s about, and he is one-of-one.

“He’s had a tremendous impact on this team and it certainly is not exclusive to just getting a sack today. He’s made a tremendous impact. He’s a guy that everybody looks at and they say, ‘Watching the way that he moves — if you just mimic and emulate that, we’ll be in good shape.’”

Donald has tallied 15 total tackles with four tackles for loss, four QB hits, and now 1.5 sacks since returning from his retirement at the start of the season.

Robert Pattinson's Batman Movie Franchise Is Officially Finished

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

No, I didn’t forget about The Dark Knight Rises

With Robert Pattinson confirmed to be the next James Bond, one of the biggest takeaways is that Matt Reeves’ The Batman series will likely end with The Batman: Part II. Pattinson is signed on for three films as Bond, and while that’s one less film than the four-film deal that Amazon MGM Studios was seeking for the other Bond contenders, it’s pretty hard to imagine that Pattinson will juggle the role of 007 with an ongoing commitment to DC — much less Battinson’s integration into the wider DCU, which some DC fans had hoped for. To be clear, nothing has been confirmed about the status of a third The Batman movie, but the latest news combined with the extreme delays facing The Batman Part II feel like a pretty clear indication that the Batmobile has hit a dead end and will need yet another reboot.

However, the loss is even bigger than you might realize, as this will be the third time in a row that Batman fans will be robbed of a good Batman trilogy.

Tim Burton’s 1989 film Batman, of course, revolutionized the character of Batman and comic book movies as a whole. Before then, the general public’s awareness of Batman was mostly associated with the campy, comedic Adam West Batman, but Burton changed that and presented a dark, heavily stylized version starring Michael Keaton. He then followed it up with Batman Returns, which many still consider to be one of the best Batman films ever.

Warner Bros., however, felt it was too dark and got rid of Burton for the third film, replacing him with Joel Schumacher. Keaton followed Burton out the door, not wanting to continue as Batman without him. Batman Forever, starring Val Kilmer, was the result, and while that film has its defenders, it felt like a different franchise and a different Batman altogether. The same can be said for Batman and Robin.

Then in 2005 came Batman Begins, directed by Christopher Nolan and starring Christian Bale. Taking a far more grounded approach to the character, Batman Begins turned Batman’s origin into an epic film saga, where it had only been relegated to brief flashbacks in previous films. Nolan topped himself with The Dark Knight by putting Batman in the center of a gritty crime drama with Heath Ledger’s Oscar-winning turn as the Joker. For a second time, a Batman film revolutionized comic book films. Nearly 20 years later, many still cite The Dark Knight as the greatest comic book movie ever made.

Unfortunately, Nolan’s third Batman film wasn’t nearly as good as the first two. Perhaps because of Ledger’s untimely death before the release of The Dark Knight, Nolan at first seemed disinterested in a third film, and it is generally understood that he had to rework the story he had in mind to remove the Joker from its narrative. Regardless of the reasons why, The Dark Knight Rises is a deeply uneven, disorganized film that most view as the low point of Nolan’s Batman, if not an outright bad film (I tend to think it is quite bad).

After Nolan’s trilogy concluded, Ben Affleck became the next Batman, in both Batman v. Superman: Dawn of Justice as well as the various versions of Justice League. Regardless of what you think of these films — I personally find them insufferable — they were deeply divisive. Despite an Affleck-led Batman film being rumored for a time, we never got him in any standalone films, much less three of them.

In 2022, The Batman offered new hope. While some were skeptical of Pattinson as Batman, he proved to be a really solid take on the Dark Knight, which prioritized Batman’s skills as a detective, something missing from previous Batman films. He was complemented by Paul Dano’s frightening take as the Riddler and Colin Farrell’s scene-stealing Penguin.

Reeves also presented the best-ever take on Gotham City. While Burton’s Gotham was bold and interesting, neither of his films made Gotham feel like a real city. Nolan’s Gotham, with its unconcealed usage of Chicago and New York City, had little personality at all. But Reeves’ Gotham felt like a broken city that desperately needed a Batman to come to its rescue, with production combining parts of Chicago and Scotland to give the city a more naturalistic, gothic vibe. The film’s nearly three-hour runtime only assisted in the viewer’s immersion into Reeves’ Gotham.

But three hours in Reeves’ Gotham still wasn’t enough. The Batman Part II, which seems to be telling a story with Two-Face as a villain, was delayed for years due to a personal issue going on with Matt Reeves. Filming finally began in June, but in early October, Reeves had to step away, most likely for the same family issue. Steven Soderbergh then stepped in to finish the filming Reeves began, which is currently ongoing. While I applaud Reeves for prioritizing his family over a film, this means that it will be six years between The Batman and its sequel and it's hard to imagine that Skydance, the new owners of DC Studios, will want to keep Batman on the shelf for that long again, nor do I think they’ll want to schedule around Pattinson’s Bond films.

And, while it never seemed like a real possibility, I was still hoping that Pattinson would get folded into what James Gunn and Peter Safran had been building for the DCU if only because I don’t think it's all that easy to keep finding great guys to be Batman, and while he wasn’t quite as good as Bale — or even Keaton — Pattinson truly is/was a great Batman. It’s just too bad that his take on the character will be cut short before we get a proper trilogy.

Why Casting Robert Pattinson as James Bond Is a High-Risk, High-Reward Gamble for Denis Villeneuve

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Throughout his 64 years in movies, James Bond, the hard-living superspy, has been the star of the 007 franchise. But by granting Robert Pattinson a license to kill, the producers of the next Bond film are making a bet that one of the most famous men on the planet can slide into a role shaped by the likes of Sean Connery, Daniel Craig, Roger Moore, and Pierce Brosnan without bringing any Edward Cullen-sized baggage.

It’s quite a gamble. After all, Connery, Craig, Timothy Dalton, and poor, delusional George Lazenby were relative unknowns when they were tapped to play the secret agent, and Brosnan and Moore were TV-famous but unproven as film stars. That’s not the case with Pattinson, who has been one of the most recognizable people on the planet for two decades, and who is currently anchoring another massive franchise in “Batman.” So why did director Denis Villeneuve, producers David Heyman and Amy Pascal, and Amazon MGM Studios decide to hand over the keys to the Aston Martin to such a prominent and expensive candidate? Related Stories ’14th’ Trailer: Ava DuVernay Unpacks the Necessity of Another Essential Constitutional Amendment Robert Pattinson Is the Next James Bond: Denis Villeneuve Finds Star for His 007 Movie

Well, some of it has to do with Villeneuve’s comfort level. He’s worked with Pattinson on “Dune: Part Three” and knows what the mercurial star can bring to a role. Clearly, he wants to build a darker, brooding Bond. Then, there’s also the need to have insurance. Amazon is launching a new iteration of 007 into a world that looks very different than it did in 2019 when the last chapter, “No Time to Die,” was conceived and shot (it wouldn’t be released until 2021 thanks to COVID). We’ve lived through #MeToo, a pandemic, wars in Ukraine and the Middle East, the fraying of the Western World and, with it, the “special relationship” Bond has sworn to protect. From the start, Amazon was interested in picking a higher-profile actor to outfit in one of Bond’s signature tuxedos. Before the search began in earnest last May, executives were high on the idea of Jacob Elordi.

Plus, some of the other finalists, a group that included Callum Turner, Jack Lowden, Josh O’Connor, and Nicholas Hoult, lacked the kind of sex appeal and danger that the part required. Hoult apparently impressed executives with his audition, but came up short, as he did when he was in contention for Batman (losing out to Pattinson) and Superman (getting Lex Luthor as a consolation prize). He remains very well liked in the industry. With a salary estimated at roughly $2.5 million, Hoult would have been a cheaper option than Pattinson, who one industry source speculated would cost north of $10 million to hire.

But the higher price tag may be worth it. This isn’t just the first Bond to be released under Amazon’s ownership — it’s the first to be produced without the Broccoli family at the helm. The keepers of the 007 flame sold their creative control to Amazon in 2025, leaving the series without a guiding force. Both Heyman and Pascal are old hands, having overseen the casting of the Harry Potter and Spider-Man franchises, respectively, but those are still big shoes to fill.

What’s in it for Pattinson? Well, a chance to put his stamp on an iconic role, as well as an opportunity to reunite with Villeneuve, one of the hottest directors in Hollywood. His commercial bona fides have also, arguably, never been higher. Not only has he shown he can anchor a superhero film with “The Batman” and its upcoming sequel, but he’s also opened more idiosyncratic fare, such as the recent A24 hits “The Drama” and “Primetime.” Clearly, he can bring in the younger audiences driving the box office turnaround while retaining a kind of indie cool that could take Bond in a new and exciting direction. Will he be our perviest 007?

Plus, the recent shake-up at DC Studios, with James Gunn and Peter Safran replaced by former Marvel executive Jeremy Latcham, may spell trouble for Matt Reeves’ “The Batman” series. Although it was conceived as a trilogy, the sequel was delayed multiple times as Reeves dealt with personal matters, forcing him to leave production last month, with Steven Soderbergh stepping in to finish shooting. DC may not want to wait as long to make another installment, and there’s a sense that Latcham may want something brighter and more mainstream than Reeves’ moody, hard PG-13 take on The Dark Knight.

But Pattinson better get ready for a lot more scrutiny than the questions that greeted his casting as Bruce Wayne. “With Batman, there’s a mask to hide behind,” said one industry source. “With Bond, your face is front and center.”

Even Craig, widely considered to be the best Bond since Connery, faced fierce blowback when his casting was announced. He was too blonde, too unconventionally handsome. A website, CraigNotBond, sprang up where fans vented their displeasure.

But when “Casino Royale” debuted in 2006, and Craig showed that he was perfectly capable of kicking ass and ordering his martini shaken, not stirred, the haters were quickly silenced. Will Pattinson be able to pull off the same stunt? It’s a high-risk, high-reward gamble. Just the kind of bet James Bond lives to make.

Robert Pattinson’s James Bond Announcement Is Already Brewing Some Watch Drama

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Throughout the years-long process of casting the new James Bond, I tried to follow the watches to an answer. When Aaron Taylor-Johnson joined the Omega family in May 2025, it felt like watching the 007 Vatican spit white smoke from its steeple. Just this past weekend, fans were still pinning red strings on their bulletin boards between Jack Lowden and the MI6 superspy when images resurfaced of the Slow Horses star wearing Omega. Since 1995’s Goldeneye, Bond has been an Omega man—a relationship later canonized in one of cinema’s most iconic line readings, when Daniel Craig confirmed his brand of choice in three stretched-out syllables: Oh-Mee-Guh.

If, like me, you were only basing your predictions on what the reported pool of potential Bonds wore on their wrists, Robert Pattinson would have been an easy name to cross off. The newly announced 007 only signed on as an ambassador for the Swiss luxury brand Jaeger-LeCoultre two short months ago! When I interviewed Pattinson about the appointment over the summer, he was effusive about his new partner: “I very rarely even wear watches, but when I tried it on, I was like, ‘That’s my favorite watch.’ I wanted to just buy it.”

Now, in addition to juggling his other iconic role as Batman, Pattinson will presumably need to balance his existing commitment to JLC with his new gig’s long-held ties to Omega. (Reps for both brands did not immediately respond to requests for comment.)

During our conversation earlier this summer, Pattinson revealed what he looks for in a watch. He caught the collecting bug in March, when he wore JLC’s Master Control Chronometre Perpetual Calendar to the Oscars afterparties. “All these other actors with their fancy watches on asked me, ‘Hey, what are you wearing?’” Pattinson recalls. “It was one of those moments where I’m like, ‘It’s kind of badass having this thing on.’”

If it’s attention-grabbing badass watches Pattinson is after, he’s in good hands with Omega. The brand is best known for supplying the watches Neil Armstrong and Buzz Aldrin wore when they landed on the moon in 1969. Omega has been happy to create special versions of the Speedmaster for its current partners to dial up its profile. And, of course, the Swiss house continues to expand its Bond-inspired offerings: The maker recently added a “Spectre” version of the Seamaster, 007’s preferred model, to the lineup. And whenever Omega needs to drum up buzz around soon-to-be-released models, they tend to show up on former Bond Craig’s wrists during public appearances.

Speculators trying to pin down the next Bond would have had better luck if they’d read some less recent watch tea leaves. Back in 2020, Christopher Nolan released Tenet, his inscrutable but enthralling time-warp thriller in which Pattinson plays a mysterious, dapper, and charming spy involved in a high-stakes mission to save the world—about as good a preview of what the 40-year-old actor will look like as 007 as you’ll find in his filmography. And the watch on his wrist in that flick? An Omega.

We’ve Got a New James Bond, and He’s No Stranger to Major Film Franchises

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Robert Pattinson is staying booked and busy.

It’s safe to say actor Robert Pattinson is on a generational run, starring in films The Odyssey, The Drama, Die My Love and Primetime –  just this year alone. Having previously played Edward Cullen in The Twilight Saga and Bruce Wayne in Matt Reeves’ The Batman (and gearing up for a role in Dune: Part III this December), he’s no stranger to major film franchises.

And now he’s adding one more major role to his portfolio: James Bond.

Amazon MGM Studios named the English actor the new 007 on Monday, ending the hunt to replace Daniel Craig, who played Bond from 2006 to 2021 in five films: Casino Royale, Quantum of Solace, Skyfall, Spectre and No Time to Die.

Read More: Ready to Watch a James Bond Movie? These Are My Top Recommendations for You

Several other British A-list actors were speculated to be in the running for the role, including Jack Lowden, Callum Turner and Josh O’Connor, but it seems Amazon MGM Studios is banking on a big-name actor with a proven record of box-office success for their lead. Pattinson’s connection to director Denis Villeneuve (also director of the Dune trilogy) likely played a role in the decision, too.

“To say this role is a dream come true is an understatement,” Pattinson said in a statement. “I couldn’t be more excited about this partnership and the work ahead with this fantastic team.”

Pattinson is the eighth actor to play James Bond. The full list of actors who have played Bond on the big screen includes Sean Connery (1962-1983), David Niven for one film in 1967, George Lazenby for one film in 1969, Roger Moore (1973-1985), Timothy Dalton (1987-1989), Pierce Brosnan (1995-2002) and Craig (2006-2021).

The next Bond film has not yet been titled and is not expected to begin filming until 2027, with a 2028 release date. Steven Knight (Peaky Blinders) has been brought on as the screenwriter, with Amy Pascal (Project Hail Mary) and David Heyman (Harry Potter series) producing and Tanya Lapointe (Dune trilogy) executive producing.

Amazon formally closed its acquisition of MGM Studios in 2022 for $8.45 billion, giving it the rights to distribute all James Bond films. In 2025, Amazon MGM Studios announced a joint venture with longtime James Bond franchise producers Michael G. Wilson and Barbara Broccoli to remain co-owners of the franchise, while ultimately giving Amazon MGM Studios creative control.

“Few films carry the kind of anticipation that comes with James Bond, and we have strived to honor the passionate fans, the legacy and the future of this iconic character,” Courtenay Valenti, head of film at Amazon MGM Studios, said. “We are thrilled to officially welcome Robert Pattinson as our 007 and are confident he will bring something truly special to this role. Robert, Denis, Amy, David and Tanya are embarking on an amazing adventure together.”

Candace Cameron Bure and husband Valeri Bure hinted at the steamy secret to their longtime marriage, with him joking that their three kids wouldn't want to hear what it is.

Candace Cameron Bure knows how to keep a full house with husband Valeri Bure.

The Fuller House alum and former NHL star teased what they believe to be the secret to their 30-year marriage—and it’s not exactly Tanner-approved.

“Is it PG or not?” Valeri quipped to Us Weekly in a joint interview with Candace published Nov. 13. “I have a couple secrets, and our kids do not want to hear those secrets.”

As for Candace—who shares kids Natasha, 28, Lev, 26, and Maksim, 24, with Valeri—the 50-year-old offered a more G-rated answer, adding, “He’s funny. Makes me laugh.”

And being married for so long also means growing and changing together, an aspect of their relationship that Valeri is particularly grateful for.

“That’s the fun part about it, is that our journeys continue to go forward,” he shared, “and not be like, ‘Oh my gosh, OK, you’re the same person that you were 30 years ago.’”

Candace has noticed those changes happening within herself, including while she filmed season five of Fox’s Special Forces: World's Toughest Test—which she won alongside Collin Gosselin and Alexia Umansky. And then I met my husband at 18 and he was 20. And my husband leads. He’s a professional athlete and he’s an Olympian.”

But once she became aware of it, she wanted to change that within herself.

“I do have a spirit in me and so at this age, I really wanted to see if I could push it,” she said. “I want to step up.”

New Regency Sues Michael Mann to Retain Rights to ‘Heat’

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The production company is challenging the director's attempts to reclaim rights to the screenplay.

New Regency is suing Michael Mann to reassert its ownership over Heat ahead of the high-profile follow-up to the 1995 crime drama classic.

In a lawsuit filed on Friday in California federal court, New Regency argues that Mann can’t exploit a provision in copyright law that allows creators to claw back ownership of their works after a certain period of time. It seeks a court order clarifying that it retains the rights to the screenplay for Heat.

“The Screenplay is not subject to termination,” states the complaint. Related Stories Movies Adam Driver Confirms 'Heat 2' Villain Role, Shares Michael Mann Update After Accidental Shooting General News Michael Mann Accidentally Shoots Himself at L.A. Gun Range

In a statement, a spokesperson for Mann said the director is “confident that the courts will vindicate Michael Mann’s statutory rights under the Copyright Act.”

“This filing has to do with original and personal writing Michael Mann did on multiple screenplays during the 1980s and which evolved into the iconic 1995 film Heat that Mann wrote, produced and directed,” the spokesperson added. “After 40 years Michael Mann is exercising the right of authors to reclaim their work and in this case Michael Mann’s original creation.”

The filing comes ahead Mann preparing to film Heat 2, a sequel starring Al Pacino, Robert De Niro and the late Val Kilmer. The upcoming film is based on Mann’s 2022 novel of the same name, which serves as both a prequel and sequel to the original.

This filing has to do with original and personal writing Michael Mann did on multiple screenplays during the 1980s and which evolved into the iconic 1995 film Heat that Mann wrote, produced and directed.

After 40 years Michael Mann is exercising the right of authors to reclaim their work and in this case Michael Mann’s original creation.

We will vigorously defend this action and are confident that the courts will vindicate Michael Mann’s statutory rights under the Copyright Act.

For clarification, this is unrelated to production of Heat 2 which will begin shooting on January 19.

Collin Gosselin Reacts to Kate Gosselin's “Passive Aggressive” Posts

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Kate Gosselin's son Collin Gosselin urged the Jon & Kate Plus 8 alum to "come forward, take accountability" for the alleged abuse he suffered during childhood in an exclusive interview with E! News.

Collin Gosselin would like his mom Kate Gosselin to step out from the shadows.

One month after publishing his memoir In the Shadow of Eight: Surviving the Reality of My Childhood, the 22-year-old is urging his mother to "come forward, take accountability" for the alleged abuse he suffered during his childhood rather than making what he calls “passive aggressive" social media comments.

"Accountability is overall a great thing," Collin—one of Kate's sextuplets with ex-husband Jon Gosselin—said in an exclusive interview with E! News. "When you're wrong, admitting you're wrong, it's OK. It doesn't make you weak. It makes you the bigger person to say, 'I was wrong.'"

"One thing that I really would hope for my mother to do is to take accountability," he continued. "If you stand behind what you say, you can make it a statement, but you're just going on your TikTok comments. It's very passive and it looks strange."

E! News has reached out to Kate's rep for comment on allegations in his memoir but hasn’t heard back. In the past, she called Collin's allegations of abuse “ridiculous” and defended her decision to send him to a mental health clinic as a tween.

"My son Collin, whom I love with all my heart, has received multiple psychiatric diagnoses over the years," Kate wrote on Instagram in 2023. "For the safety of myself, his brothers and sisters and for his own well-being, he was placed in a facility following years of outpatient treatment which proved insufficient for his needs."

Though the former TLC star has not released an official statement addressing Collin's memoir, she did respond to several social media comments criticizing the book last month.

"I know logical ppl or anyone who has dealt with mental illness understands what's going on here," Kate wrote on TikTok in October, after one fan accused Collin of spreading lies with his memoir. Under another comment, she penned, "I know who I am and my kids know who I truly am and to me, that's all that matters."

And while Collin has been estranged from his mom for nearly 10 years, he believes there is still a chance for reconciliation if accountability is involved. After all, it's been a part of his healing journey to own up to his mistakes.

"There are a lot of things I messed up on in life," the Special Forces: America's Toughest Test winner shared in his E! interview. "There are things I've done where I was like, 'I need to take accountability for that.'"

"Some people can't do that at who knows how many years old, but to learn that lesson young, I'm grateful that I feel strongly about how I do on accountability," he continued. "To stand back, to humble yourself, to say that, 'I could've done this better, I could have listened better, I could have been a better human being'—that's OK to say."

Collin added, "It makes you strong to be able to recognize that."

In the Shadow of Eight: Surviving the Reality of My Childhood is out now. The memoir, a New York Times No. 1 best seller since its Oct. 13 release, is currently nominated for two Goodreads Choice Awards.

Collin Gosselin Says His Mom Separated Him From His Siblings

On a 2015 episode of Kate Plus 8—the Jon & Kate Plus 8 spinoff Kate Gosselin  led following her 2009 divorce from Jon Gosselin—TLC viewers watched as twins Mady Gosselin and Cara Gosselin moved into their own rooms in Kate's 7,500-square-foot estate, while their younger sisters Leah Gosselin, Alexis Gosselin and Hannah Gosselin inhabited a shared upstairs room and brothers Collin Gosselin, Joel Gosselin and Aaden Gosselin made over a basement space.

"It was like a slumber party every night," Collin recounts of that first week in his memoir Shadow of Eight: Surviving the Reality of My Childhood. "Then, one day, I was told by my mom I’d be moving out. An air mattress appeared in the unfinished part of the basement, shoved into the far back corner like an afterthought."

Coming on the heels of him being cut out of weekend visits with his dad (he was told Jon didn't want to see him; Jon was told the opposite) and frequently isolated (he recalls one night where he was sent to bed while his siblings watched TV with Kate), having to live next to their deep freezers felt particularly cold.

"I was part of a plan," he notes of the punishments, he attributed to his unwillingness to play ball on camera "I just didn’t know what it was."

E! News reached out to reps for Kate and TLC about the accusations in Collin's book and has not heard back. However, this past June, Kate referred to her son's accusations as "ridiculous" in the comment section of her TikTok page.

Collin Gosselin Claims His Parents Kate Gosselin and Jon Gosselin Were Offered Millions to Stay Married

As Jon and Kate's marriage began to unravel, claims Collin, Dr. Phil host Phil McGraw was teaming with TLC "to negotiate a deal to keep their marriage together for cameras."

So, when the couple shared their plans to divorce in January 2009, writes Collin, "the network responded by offering my parents $2 million to stay in the marriage and pretend the relationship was intact for the sake of the show’s longevity." Turning down the offer, Jon told the Daily Mail in 2019, "I couldn't live with myself. I couldn't live a lie."

E! News reached out to reps for Kate and TLC, but they did not respond. They also declined to comment on Daily Mail's previous story. A rep for McGraw called the allegations "false" in a statement to Us Weekly. Noting the talk show host didn't have "any direct or indirect interest, financial or otherwise," in the series, the rep added, "Neither Dr. Phil, nor anyone associated with his show ever facilitated, encouraged, participated in or knew anything about any payments to the Gosselins by the other network as alleged by their son Collin, who Dr. Phil has never met."

Collin Gosselin Says He Spent 70 Percent of His Time Locked in His Basement Bedroom

According to Collin, some 14 complaints were made against his mom to Children and Youth Services, one of them coming after he turned up to his fourth-grade classroom with a noticeably injured ear.

Not long after, "my mom pulled me from school," he wrote. "She told me it was temporary. That it was for my own good. That people were 'asking questions' and we needed to keep things calm for a while. I didn’t argue or ask how long. I'd learned that asking questions made things worse."

Collin said he was also banned from playing with the family's LEGO sets. But perhaps the biggest change, Collin alleged, was the walls Kate had built around his basement air mattress with two security cameras he had to speak through if he needed to be let out to use the bathroom.

"Most days, I spent about 70 percent of my time locked in that room," writes Collin. "The rest was split between meeting with my teacher, eating alone without my siblings, cleaning up after everyone or pretending to be happy when the cameras came around. That was my life."

Collin Gosselin Claims Mom Kate Gosselin Told His Siblings He Was a Bad Kid

To explain why Collin was no longer attending school with them or even joining the rest of the family to eat dinner, Kate would tell his siblings he was troubled, Collin writes.

"'Collin needs more help than you kids,' she would say right in front of me," he details in his book. "'You and your friends at school aren't bad like your brother.' They were repeatedly told that I was unpredictable, violent, beyond help. They grew up around me as if I was a cautionary tale. They were children themselves. How could they know to doubt her?"

Kate previously told People that Collin received "multiple psychiatric diagnoses" and inpatient psychiatric treatment in response to "unpredictable and violent behavior." But Jon claimed to the Daily Mail in 2019 that Collin had no known diagnosed medical condition other than common ADHD.

Collin Gosselin Alleges Mom Kate Gosselin Once Bound His Wrists and Ankles With Zip Ties

Having tired of his basement existence by age 11, Collin plotted his escape, using his twin-size air mattress to climb out the tiny basement window. But with nowhere to go—"It had been almost two years since I'd seen my dad"—he returned home.

Once there, his mattress was gone, as were the few books he had. Then, Collin detailed, Kate allegedly came in with zip ties for his wrists and ankles.

"Once I was bound, she grabbed me by my wrists, pulled me up into a standing position and then threw me down, slamming my tailbone against the hard floor," Collin alleges, adding she told him he was a burden to the family. "She went on, calling me crazy, dangerous and other names nobody should ever call a child, things that stuck long after the pain faded."

A rep for Kate didn’t respond to E! News' request for comment. But her attorney previously told The Sun, "There's always two sides to every story and when things take place in the past, it's hard to determine because people have differing recollections and perspectives."

However, his take, he added, was "if Kate did the things that Collin is accusing Kate of, she would have been investigated by the authorities and prosecuted."

Collin Gosselin Recounts Being Physically Restrained and Sedated at a Mental Health Clinic

After his escape attempt, Collin claims he was transported to Fairmount Behavioral Health System mere days before his 12th birthday. Throughout his 21-month stay in the mental health clinic—where most kids stayed just days or weeks, he claims—he was given daily medication. "My brain had started feeling soft and cloudy, like someone had turned a dimmer switch down," he details. "I found myself zoning out, feeling like a zombie."

During one incident, 18 months into his stay, Collin alleges he was physically restrained and injected with a medication he dubbed "booty juice" after asking administrators why he was still there. After another, a staffer bent his wrist in such a way that "a violent, searing pain shot up my arm, so intense it made my knees buckle." (E! News reached out to Fairmount Behavioral Health System, but has not heard back.)

Throughout his nearly two-year stay, Collin says his mom visited once.

Collin Gosselin Says He Smuggled a Letter to Dad Jon Gosselin to Get Out of the Mental Health Clinic

With his mom and siblings no longer taking his calls from Fairmount, said Collin, he hatched a plan to reconnect with his dad. After hitting it off with one of his roommates, Collin confided in him about his reality star past.

Writing a letter to his father in crayon while hiding in a bathroom stall, Collin claimed he asked his roommate to find a way to deliver it. Soon after, he was discharged to a residential facility where he was able to once-again attend school and slowly weaned off his medications.

It was there that he connected with his dad for the first time, Jon sharing in that initial phone call that he had plans to bring him home.

As Jon later detailed to Collin—who ended up moving in with him, sister Hannah and Jon's then-girlfriend Colleen Conrad—he had spent years trying to track him down. Then he received Collin's letter and filed an emergency petition with the court to modify custody.

Collin Gosselin Says He Hasn't Seen Most of His Siblings Since He Was 15

Other than Hannah, Collin has only seen his siblings once since leaving Kate's home at age 11—a court-mandated visit just before his 15th birthday. "It was a way to keep the kids together, despite being split between two households," Collin reasons, noting the meet-up started out awkwardly before they all teamed up to best the amusement center's games.

But, as Leah, Alexis, Joel and Aaden moved toward the exit, "No one turned back," Collin recounts. "And I haven't seen them since."

Collin Gosselin Reveals His Hope to Have Kids

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In the Shadow of Eight: Surviving the Reality of My Childhood author Collin Gosselin exclusively shared with E! News what his hopes are for his future, including where he stands on having kids.

Collin Gosselin is looking to his future.

While the Special Forces season five winner is grateful for what he’s achieved in his life as an advocate for children and those in need, he does have an idea of what he thinks could be better than this.

“The one thing that could top this beautiful mission I'm on is probably being a father,” Gosselin exclusively shared with E! News, “but I feel so young. I've already found something so great and so meaningful, and to that I am very fortunate.”

And so much of that mission crystallized for the 22-year-old in his memoir In the Shadow of Eight: Surviving the Reality of My Childhood—which is currently a New York Times No.1 best seller and has been nominated for favorite memoir and audiobook in the Goodreads Choice Awards.

The release of Gosselin’s book has allowed him to reach new audiences, many of whom have been moved by his vulnerability as he discussed growing up in the public eye on the reality series Jon & Kate Plus 8 with parents Jon Gosselin and Kate Gosselin, as well as his fellow sextuplets Alexis, Leah, Joel, Hannah and Aaden, and 26-year-old twin sisters Mady and Cara.

“The coolest, the most meaningful messages I've got from people are them sharing their stories with me,” Gosselin told E!. “Talking about their dynamics with their parents and their siblings and their childhoods. And people I've never met before having that vulnerability? That's hard to do.”

Even he himself was nervous about being so vulnerable regarding his life in his memoir, adding, “That's scary to do.”

“But now, they're coming back to me, they're messaging me and they're sharing their stories with me,” he said, “and I'm like, Wow.’ I know it doesn't sound crazy, but to me it's really crazy because I know how scary it is to wanna open up.”

And that fact that so many people felt comfortable “choosing to do that with” him felt incredibly empowering and helped Gosselin learn how true emotional healing can happen.

“I’ve started this cycle of people choosing to talk about their emotions, to be vulnerable, and to talk about things that they're scared to talk about,” he explained, “and that's how healing happens. That's how we heal, is by talking about things.”

And despite a difficult childhood, Gosselin found that it inspired the mission he’s now on, saying, “The greatest thing is that what has happened in my past—as hard as it was—it has given me a purpose to do something greater with my life.”

For a look at all the difficult moments Gosselin wrote about in his memoir, keep reading.

Collin Gosselin Says His Mom Separated Him From His Siblings

On a 2015 episode of Kate Plus 8—the Jon & Kate Plus 8 spinoff Kate Gosselin  led following her 2009 divorce from Jon Gosselin—TLC viewers watched as twins Mady Gosselin and Cara Gosselin moved into their own rooms in Kate's 7,500-square-foot estate, while their younger sisters Leah Gosselin, Alexis Gosselin and Hannah Gosselin inhabited a shared upstairs room and brothers Collin Gosselin, Joel Gosselin and Aaden Gosselin made over a basement space.

"It was like a slumber party every night," Collin recounts of that first week in his memoir Shadow of Eight: Surviving the Reality of My Childhood. "Then, one day, I was told by my mom I’d be moving out. An air mattress appeared in the unfinished part of the basement, shoved into the far back corner like an afterthought."

Coming on the heels of him being cut out of weekend visits with his dad (he was told Jon didn't want to see him; Jon was told the opposite) and frequently isolated (he recalls one night where he was sent to bed while his siblings watched TV with Kate), having to live next to their deep freezers felt particularly cold.

"I was part of a plan," he notes of the punishments, he attributed to his unwillingness to play ball on camera "I just didn’t know what it was."

E! News reached out to reps for Kate and TLC about the accusations in Collin's book and has not heard back. However, this past June, Kate referred to her son's accusations as "ridiculous" in the comment section of her TikTok page.

Collin Gosselin Claims His Parents Kate Gosselin and Jon Gosselin Were Offered Millions to Stay Married

As Jon and Kate's marriage began to unravel, claims Collin, Dr. Phil host Phil McGraw was teaming with TLC "to negotiate a deal to keep their marriage together for cameras."

So, when the couple shared their plans to divorce in January 2009, writes Collin, "the network responded by offering my parents $2 million to stay in the marriage and pretend the relationship was intact for the sake of the show’s longevity." Turning down the offer, Jon told the Daily Mail in 2019, "I couldn't live with myself. I couldn't live a lie."

E! News reached out to reps for Kate and TLC, but they did not respond. They also declined to comment on Daily Mail's previous story. A rep for McGraw called the allegations "false" in a statement to Us Weekly. Noting the talk show host didn't have "any direct or indirect interest, financial or otherwise," in the series, the rep added, "Neither Dr. Phil, nor anyone associated with his show ever facilitated, encouraged, participated in or knew anything about any payments to the Gosselins by the other network as alleged by their son Collin, who Dr. Phil has never met."

Collin Gosselin Says He Spent 70 Percent of His Time Locked in His Basement Bedroom

According to Collin, some 14 complaints were made against his mom to Children and Youth Services, one of them coming after he turned up to his fourth-grade classroom with a noticeably injured ear.

Not long after, "my mom pulled me from school," he wrote. "She told me it was temporary. That it was for my own good. That people were 'asking questions' and we needed to keep things calm for a while. I didn’t argue or ask how long. I'd learned that asking questions made things worse."

Collin said he was also banned from playing with the family's LEGO sets. But perhaps the biggest change, Collin alleged, was the walls Kate had built around his basement air mattress with two security cameras he had to speak through if he needed to be let out to use the bathroom.

"Most days, I spent about 70 percent of my time locked in that room," writes Collin. "The rest was split between meeting with my teacher, eating alone without my siblings, cleaning up after everyone or pretending to be happy when the cameras came around. That was my life."

Collin Gosselin Claims Mom Kate Gosselin Told His Siblings He Was a Bad Kid

To explain why Collin was no longer attending school with them or even joining the rest of the family to eat dinner, Kate would tell his siblings he was troubled, Collin writes.

"'Collin needs more help than you kids,' she would say right in front of me," he details in his book. "'You and your friends at school aren't bad like your brother.' They were repeatedly told that I was unpredictable, violent, beyond help. They grew up around me as if I was a cautionary tale. They were children themselves. How could they know to doubt her?"

Kate previously told People that Collin received "multiple psychiatric diagnoses" and inpatient psychiatric treatment in response to "unpredictable and violent behavior." But Jon claimed to the Daily Mail in 2019 that Collin had no known diagnosed medical condition other than common ADHD.

Collin Gosselin Alleges Mom Kate Gosselin Once Bound His Wrists and Ankles With Zip Ties

Having tired of his basement existence by age 11, Collin plotted his escape, using his twin-size air mattress to climb out the tiny basement window. But with nowhere to go—"It had been almost two years since I'd seen my dad"—he returned home.

Once there, his mattress was gone, as were the few books he had. Then, Collin detailed, Kate allegedly came in with zip ties for his wrists and ankles.

"Once I was bound, she grabbed me by my wrists, pulled me up into a standing position and then threw me down, slamming my tailbone against the hard floor," Collin alleges, adding she told him he was a burden to the family. "She went on, calling me crazy, dangerous and other names nobody should ever call a child, things that stuck long after the pain faded."

A rep for Kate didn’t respond to E! News' request for comment. But her attorney previously told The Sun, "There's always two sides to every story and when things take place in the past, it's hard to determine because people have differing recollections and perspectives."

However, his take, he added, was "if Kate did the things that Collin is accusing Kate of, she would have been investigated by the authorities and prosecuted."

Collin Gosselin Recounts Being Physically Restrained and Sedated at a Mental Health Clinic

After his escape attempt, Collin claims he was transported to Fairmount Behavioral Health System mere days before his 12th birthday. Throughout his 21-month stay in the mental health clinic—where most kids stayed just days or weeks, he claims—he was given daily medication. "My brain had started feeling soft and cloudy, like someone had turned a dimmer switch down," he details. "I found myself zoning out, feeling like a zombie."

During one incident, 18 months into his stay, Collin alleges he was physically restrained and injected with a medication he dubbed "booty juice" after asking administrators why he was still there. After another, a staffer bent his wrist in such a way that "a violent, searing pain shot up my arm, so intense it made my knees buckle." (E! News reached out to Fairmount Behavioral Health System, but has not heard back.)

Throughout his nearly two-year stay, Collin says his mom visited once.

Collin Gosselin Says He Smuggled a Letter to Dad Jon Gosselin to Get Out of the Mental Health Clinic

With his mom and siblings no longer taking his calls from Fairmount, said Collin, he hatched a plan to reconnect with his dad. After hitting it off with one of his roommates, Collin confided in him about his reality star past.

Writing a letter to his father in crayon while hiding in a bathroom stall, Collin claimed he asked his roommate to find a way to deliver it. Soon after, he was discharged to a residential facility where he was able to once-again attend school and slowly weaned off his medications.

It was there that he connected with his dad for the first time, Jon sharing in that initial phone call that he had plans to bring him home.

As Jon later detailed to Collin—who ended up moving in with him, sister Hannah and Jon's then-girlfriend Colleen Conrad—he had spent years trying to track him down. Then he received Collin's letter and filed an emergency petition with the court to modify custody.

Collin Gosselin Says He Hasn't Seen Most of His Siblings Since He Was 15

Other than Hannah, Collin has only seen his siblings once since leaving Kate's home at age 11—a court-mandated visit just before his 15th birthday. "It was a way to keep the kids together, despite being split between two households," Collin reasons, noting the meet-up started out awkwardly before they all teamed up to best the amusement center's games.

But, as Leah, Alexis, Joel and Aaden moved toward the exit, "No one turned back," Collin recounts. "And I haven't seen them since."

Marvel Officially Debuts New Avengers Movie on Disney+ Today

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Hannah covers horror, sci-fi, and games at Collider, where she writes news and features, reviews new releases, and interviews the people who make them. She’s helping build out Collider’s gaming coverage, with Pokémon as one of her specialties. If she isn’t writing about a monster, she’s probably watching one on a tape from her VHS collection.

After three hours of watching the Avengers try to undo what Thanos did, the last thing Avengers: Endgame gives us is a dance in somebody’s living room. Steve Rogers (Chris Evans) finally gets to be with Peggy Carter (Hayley Atwell), and for a few seconds, nobody needs Captain America for anything. The Russo Brothers went back to that scene seven years later and added a knock at the door.

When Marvel brought Endgame back to theaters in September, audiences got to see who was on the other side of that door. There was more waiting in the credits, too, including a sound anyone who’s seen the first Iron Man might recognize before they realize whose face they’re looking at. Fans who saw the re-release in IMAX or an Infinity Vision-certified theater also got a separate sneak peek at Avengers: Doomsday.

Marvel Studios’ Avengers Endgame: Encore is now streaming exclusively on Disney+. The extended cut arrived on the streaming platform today, November 13, with updated color grading and about four minutes of new footage connecting the 2019 movie to Avengers: Doomsday. Disney+ confirms the added scenes are included in the streaming release. The premium-format sneak peek was advertised separately for theaters.

Four villains, and four arguments the films were honest enough to actually let them make. Here is the one you lean towards, the part of it that holds up, and the exact point where it stops holding up.

You align with the one who was right about the problem and wrong about the solution. Killmonger is the most persuasive villain Marvel has produced because the film never pretends his anger came from nowhere.

You align with the protector, which is the most understandable and most dangerous position on this list. Namor is not chasing power or revenge, he is doing whatever keeps his people hidden, and he has been doing it for centuries.

You align with the patient one, the only man on this list with no powers, no army and no hurry. Zemo lost his family in Sokovia and then spent years working out that you do not fight the Avengers, you introduce them to each other's secrets.

You align with the one who decided the answer was arithmetic. Thanos is not angry and not grieving in any way he will admit to, he simply reached a conclusion and never once revisited it, which is precisely what makes him frightening.

Spoilers for the new Encore footage follow.When Peggy answers the door, it’s Tom Hiddleston’s Loki. He tells her he can help both of them, and “both” is the part audiences keep coming back to. Steve spent years trying to get back to Peggy, and the first person to interrupt their dance makes his pitch to her. It’s an especially good use of Loki, whose own second chance came courtesy of the Avengers’ time heist. They gave him an opening to grab the Tesseract and run, setting his Disney+ series in motion. Now he’s on the doorstep of the life Steve went back in time to have.

The first credits scene picks up with Mark Ruffalo’s Bruce Banner after Spider-Man: Brand New Day. He’s recording himself in an underground bunker, explaining how he plans to keep the Hulk under control when the feed glitches. Robert Downey Jr.’s Doctor Doom speaks from off-screen. There’s a flash of green, the Hulk roars, and a magical sigil is all that’s left in the cell. At the TVA, Mobius, Casey, and O.B. watch the Fox X-Men universe and another Earth fire missiles at each other as they collide. The alerts keep coming, and the count of lost universes climbs into the thousands.

Then comes the sound of Tony Stark hammering out his first Iron Man mask. That’s a hell of a sound to hear at the end of Endgame, especially with Downey Jr. back in the picture. You know whose face you expect to see, but this time, the last face the movie leaves you with is Doom’s.

Avengers Endgame: Encore is streaming on Disney+. Avengers: Doomsday opens in theaters December 18.

Sony’s Tom Rothman Hopes It Will Be A ‘Brand New Day’ For ‘Spider-Man’ As Studio Launches Major Best Picture Oscar Campaign: “Are You Telling Me There Are 10 Films Better Than This One?”

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Watch out Oscar. Spider-Man is aiming to get your attention.

Sony Pictures and Marvel‘s Spider-Man: Brand New Day is about to launch an all-out campaign to land a spot in the Best Picture race at the 99th annual Academy Awards. Can the highest-grossing movie of not just this year but all time at the domestic box office (now just shy of $41 million to become the first billion-dollar grossing movie domestically), land a place among the coveted 10 the Academy names as Best Picture nominees? Tom Rothman, chairman and CEO of Sony Pictures Motion Picture Group, thinks so and has decided to put the full weight of the studio behind the effort.

“The Academy is meant to recognize greatness. So, to be honest, I think it’s greatness. I think it’s received wisdom. If there aren’t people thinking about it, we’re gonna do what we can just to make people think about it,” Rothman told me this week. “I think it’s also incumbent upon studios who make these kinds of quality commercial films to get people to think about it, and here’s what I ask people when I walk around: ‘Are you telling me that there are 10 films this year that are better than this film?’ ” Watch on Deadline

Spider-Man: Brand New Day, , the fourth entry into the MCU Tom Holland era of Spidey movies at Sony, is at 90% fresh on Rotten Tomatoes, nearly as critically acclaimed there as perceived large-scale Oscar contenders The Odyssey and Project Hail Mary which are also in the 90s ( and even some of the more arthouse-style contenders). Both of those, along with the upcoming unseen Dune: Part Three, are in Gold Derby’s projection of the current top 10 movies with best chances to land a Best Picture nomination. Spider-Man: Brand New Day is listed way down at No. 36, with a 0.46% chance of making the cut, well below The Sheep Detectives and a few notches above I Love Boosters. Pundits are basically ignoring the year’s biggest film, so we have to ask why.

Of the previous eight live-action Spider-Man films Sony has released since 2002, the only Oscar nominations they have received have been in the Visual Effects and Sound categories, the most recent nom for Visual Effects for 2001’s No Way Home. 2004’s Spider-Man 2 is the only one to actually win an Oscar, that one also for Visual Effects. On the animated front, 2018’s Spider-Man: Into the Spider-Verse won for Best Animated Feature, and its 2023 sequel was also nominated.

Not one of these 10 films has cracked the Best Picture race. Rothman feels Brand New Day can, and deserves to be the breakthrough.

“I think to be honest, it’s because there are things like Gold Derby, you know, whatever that is, that just tend to think only about the art film world, and look, I’m an art film guy. I made my bones in the art film world, I’m Mr. Searchlight, right? You know, my whole career, I love art films. But I’m a traditionalist, and I was never for expanding the number of Best Pictures from five to 10. Okay, I liked it the old way, but I believe that the very reason it was extended from five to 10 was so that the Academy would have room to recognize really quality commercial filmmaking,” he said pointing (correctly) to Christopher Nolan’s Best Picture-snubbed superhero movie The Dark Knight as the key reason.

“I think wise heads know that quality commercial filmmaking is extremely difficult, and to make a movie like this, never mind the fact that it’s the highest-grossing movie in history of America, forget that. It’s in the 90s on Rotten Tomatoes for critics, it’s in the 90s on audiences on Rotten Tomatoes. Here’s the thing: the movie’s great. I’m a biased witness, but that’s not me, that’s objective. You don’t have the results that you have, where we had people seeing the movie two and three times. You don’t do close to a billion dollars in America. But are there 10 films that are better than this film, just in pure quality? I think when you see movies like F1 and Barbie and Wicked and Bohemian Rhapsody, whatever, getting nominated, well that is the very reason that that the Academy changed to 10.”

So Sony has hired awards consultants and is making big plans. The studio plans a “robust” screening strategy starting just before Thanksgiving, including multiple FYC Q&As with filmmakers and department heads. Stars Tom Holland and Zendaya are also involved in campaigns for other 2026 contenders like The Odyssey and Dune 3 and Holland is in pre-production on a new film, so their availability going forward is not known. Rothman himself plans to host screenings and tastemaker events on both coasts.

In addition to Los Angeles and New York, there will be special screenings in London, Paris, Madrid, Copenhagen, Sydney, Rome, Mexico City, Berlin and Tokyo. And, as Deadline teased in September, the studio plans to do a big rerelease of the movie before year’s end — possibly as Deadline intimated with some unseen footage — that will also bring new attention to the film and hopefully inspire those Academy voters who have not seen the movie (and based on my conversations there are many) to see it in a theater.

“I think Academy members would be interested in this because it’s very significant from a storytelling movie. This is really the first of these kind of movies that I can think of that does not climax in a giant battle. In this movie there is no giant action sequence at the end. The climax of this movie are two young people sitting on the edge of a bed, talking to each other, and sharing their their deepest pain. This movie is melancholy in that way and beautiful,” he said. “I think people might take Spider-Man for granted, and not only that, I think they make assumptions about it. When you see the movie those assumptions are undone. It’s a very moving, quite personal film. Destin (Daniel Cretton) is a really personal filmmaker. This is the guy who made Short Term 12. That’s what I say when I encourage people to see it, you know, people of my generation. I say you may take it for granted, you may think it’s just another, but it isn’t. It’s really the first, when I think about it. I think it’s the first superhero movie that’s really a super person movie.”

To date, the only movies even remotely in the “superhero” universe to crack the Best Picture race have been 2018’s Black Panther, and 2019’s Joker, though the latter is really a drama far removed from what is expected in the genre.

So the biggest question is can Sony change the trajectory of the franchise in terms of Oscar and get the point across that the record-shattering success of this movie is for a reason? Rothman compares it to the Lord of the Rings franchise in hoping there can be recognition for a movie that has taken a known brand to, yes, a brand new day.

“The Academy members are really, really smart, and they know filmmaking,” he said. “And if they see the movie, which maybe they wouldn’t necessarily do, if they see the movie, I think they’ll recognize how extremely skillful it is, because they’ll know how hard it is to tell an intimate emotional story in that arena. But more than that, I think it’s this specific movie. It’s the work that Destin did, as I said, a true humanist director who put character and emotion first.”

Andreas Wiseman contributed to this story.
