Scotland 2-1 North Macedonia: Nations League – live

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Nations League updates from 7.45pm GMT kick-off

Live scores | Follow us on BlueSky | And email Scott

Scotland didn’t play brilliantly. But they weren’t flat-out awful either, given the amount of impressive work North Macedonia keeper Stole Dimitrievski had to get through in the first half. John McGinn scored the opener, a confident finish to take advantage of Ché Adams’ hard work in the build-up; McGinn converted the winning penalty, too, earned by Ben Gannon-Doak, whose persistence drew a mistake from Dimitrievski, the first-half hero turned second-half fall-guy. A deserved if uninspiring Scotland win, then; North Macedonia will have to take succour from their first goal of the campaign, Elmin Rastoder flicking home from a corner.

John McGinn’s double gives Sebastien Pocognoli his first taste of victory at Hampden!

90 min +3: Another long punt forward, another claim for Gunn. That should be that, though everyone knows all too well what can happen in the final moments at Hampden in November.

90 min +2: Alioski hoofs forward. High. But nobody in white is near the dropping ball other than Gunn. Sebastien Pocognoli 60 seconds closer to his first home win as Scotland boss.

90 min +1: One McGinn corner leads to a second. McBurnie wins a header but there’s no power on it whatsoever, and Dimitrievski claims.

90 min: Ferguson finally takes the free kick. It doesn’t cause North Macedonia any trouble. But then Robertson wins a throw deep down the left. It’s flung in, and the presence of McBurnie forces a corner, which McGinn will take out on the right. There will be four additional minutes.

89 min: Ferguson is booked for taking his sweet time over the free kick.

88 min: All very scrappy. McBurnie is going nowhere out on the right when Musliu clatters into him. A free kick and a chance for Scotland to run the clock down.

86 min: Bardhi clears the Scotland bar from 30 yards. A poor end to a slalom of grace and power.

85 min: Bardhi whips a vicious free kick into a packed Scotland box. Gunn claims it with authority. Great keeping. Any fumble, or any flick-on, would have surely resulted in a second equaliser.

84 min: Ferguson is penalised for handling a left-wing cross, just outside the Scotland box. Before the free kick can be taken, Doriev is replaced by Nikolov.

82 min: … but Hampden remains nervous, and North Macedonia are getting some joy down the left. Doriev chases a flick-on and gets into the box first. He strangely hesitates, allowing Ferguson to get back and shepherd him away from a shooting position. This game is far from done … like any Scotland fan needs to be told that.

80 min: Ilievski makes good down the right and wins a throw deep in Scotland territory. North Macedonia waste the field position, much to Hampden’s relief.

78 min: Elmas and Rastoder make way for Omeragikj and Stankovski.

77 min: A ball dinked down the North Macedonia right. Maleski reaches it and hooks back. Tierney nearly deflects it into the net. Nothing comes of the resulting corner. That was almost a carbon-copy of the response to Scotland’s first goal, and the home fans exhale after a fashion.

76 min: Scotland make a double change, sending on Christie and Hickey for Gannon-Doak and Gilmour.

75 min: Scotland’s tails are up again, and McTominay is found by Tierney down the left. He looks to curl one into the top right. Too high, too far right.

fMcGinn banks on Dimitrievski diving, which the keeper does. The ball’s battered down the middle, and McGinn performs his eye-glasses routine once more! Hampden roars! Again! With relief as much as joy!

73 min: Play hasn’t properly stopped since the Gannon-Doak / Dimitrievski incident. And the referee is sent over to the screen! There’s no question: Dimitrievski made contact with the winger, needlessly so as Gannon-Doak was almost certainly not going to reach the ball rolling out for a goal kick. The ref points at the spot! Hampden roars!

72 min: McGinn spins into space down the left but doesn’t have the legs to power into the box and shoot. He looks for McBurnie in the middle, but the cross is blocked and the move fizzles out. But hold on …

70 min: McBurnie releases Gannon-Doak into acres down the inside-right channel. Gannon-Doak enters the box and prepares to shoot, but then takes a heavy touch. He tries to salvage the situation by attempting to round the keeper on the outside, but goes over as the ball rolls out for a goal kick. He claims a penalty but he’s not getting one.

69 min: A chance for McGinn to shoot from the edge of the North Macedonia box … then a split-second later for McTominay … but both are undone by bobbles. A mixture of bad luck, low confidence, and Scotland’s recent dismal record at Hampden.

67 min: McBurnie comes on for Adams, who can feel sore about being hooked if he so desires. He could feasibly have recorded a hat-trick of assists in this second half. He’s had a good game.

65 min: Churlinov and Gashtarov are replaced by Elezi and Maleski.

64 min: Doriev makes good down the left, getting in behind Porteous and whistling an inviting ball through the Scotland six-yard box. Fortunately for the hosts, there’s nobody in white to tap home. Hampden is getting properly irate now, a sense of what may be unfolding here slowly sinking in.

63 min: Robertson crosses from the left. Adams holds up the ball and lays off to Gilmour, who gets a shot on target this time … but this time there’s a goalkeeper in the way.

61 min: North Macedonia are suddenly playing with more confidence, the dam finally broken. Churlinov sends a couple of crosses in from the right. Porteous clears. Doriev shovels a pass down the inside-right intended for Rastoder. Gunn claims. Hampden grumbles.

59 min: Billy Gilmour’s relief didn’t last too long, did it?

Bardhi whips the corner hard towards the near post. Rastoder rises highest and glances an unstoppable header across Gunn and into the right-hand portion of the net! What a response by the visitors, who have scored their first goal in Group B1, and their first goal in a year.

56 min: Adams should have had an assist to his name already – Oh Billy! – but he’s got one now. And it was highly impressive work, holding off two opponents yet still having the strength and guile to poke forward for McGinn. However, North Macedonia aren’t done yet, because they go upfield, win one corner, then another, and …

No longer! But Dimitrievski could do nothing about this. Graham sends a long header down the middle. Adams battles two defenders on the edge of the D, holding the ball up before poking it down the channel for McGinn, who is clear! McGinn lashes low and hard across Dimitrievski and into the bottom right. Hampden roars at last!

53 min: Robertson battles to win a corner down the left. He swings it under the bar. Dimitrievski punches clear, albeit not convincingly, and the ball pings off the top of Graham’s head and out for a goal kick. It continues to be a banner evening for the Macedonian keeper.

51 min: That shock nearly doubles as VAR has a check for a North Macedonia penalty, Doriev dinking a cross in from the left, the ball deflecting off the sliding Graham and out for a corner. Did it hit Graham’s arm, which was sticking up in the air? Thankfully no, and nothing comes of the set piece.

49 min: Hampden was preparing to roar there, but the shock of that miss has really rattled everyone. Palpable shock.

47 min: Scotland should be leading. That should have been the perfect start to the second half. If the hosts don’t go on to win this, that miss will stay with Gilmour for a long time. You could see the blood draining from the poor chap’s face as he picked himself up from the turf, knowing full well what he’s just done.

45 min 40 sec: Musliu doesn’t deal with a ball rolling down the Scotland right and air-kicks. Adams chases the loose ball and cuts back from the byline. Gilmour can’t miss from six yards … but somehow does, leaning back and blazing over the bar. That’s up there with Chris Iwelumo, I’m afraid.

North Macedonia get the second half started. No changes.

HALF TIME: Slovenia 0-0 Switzerland. The other game in Group B1 is goalless at the break. The Tartan Army will be praying for a Swiss surge in the second half, in order to keep what little remains of those promotion play-off hopes alive.

Half-time postbag. “That save from Dimitrievski is up there with one of the best reflex saves I’ve seen. It barely got a mention in BBC commentary preferring to concentrate on the shot by McTominay. But what a save” – Tony Mason

“Andy Robertson gained his first Scotland cap while a Dundee United player, during the one and only season he spent at Tannadice, 2013-14. So we’ll always have that. But we also have the memory of his last game in Tangerine, the Scottish Cup final defeat to St Johnstone. St Johnstone, ffs! I’m still not over that. The Scotland captain and now joint record cap holder has probably consoled himself with Champions League and Premiership winners medals, whereas United spent much of the last decade in the Scottish Championship and competing in the Tunnocks Teacake / Irn Bru Challenge trophy. So I hope he’s happy” – Simon McMahon

A few boos as Scotland trudge down the tunnel. The hosts haven’t been great, but Dimitrievski has been the outstanding player, so it’s swings and roundabouts.

Elmas shovels a pass down the inside-left channel for Bardhi, who spins and ripples the side netting. A big portion of Hampden thought that was in. It was close. Not sure Gunn was getting there had it been on target.

45 min: Porteous, facing his own goal with the ball bouncing down the middle of the park, and under no pressure, inexplicably batters the ball out for a corner. From which …

His shot is blocked and cleared, and though there are a couple of half-hearted claims for handball, neither the referee nor VAR show any interest whatsoever in awarding a penalty.

Dimitrievski, though!

39 min: Dimitrievski again! Adams releases McTominay down the inside-right channel and into the box. McTominay opts for power rather than a cute slot, and batters the ball straight at the keeper. Who is North Macedonia’s hero so far all right.

38 min: A simple punt down the middle releases Adams on goal. Adams reaches the edge of the box before aiming for the top left. Dimitrievski, who is putting showreel together tonight, sticks out an arm to divert the ball over the bar, a stunning reaction. Though it technically doesn’t matter, because the flag goes up for offside. But still, what a save!

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Hexed's Jodie Foster detailed the parenting lesson she's learned as mom to sons Charles “Charlie” Bernard Foster, 28, and Christopher “Kit” Bernard Foster, 25, in an exclusive E! News interview.

Jodie Foster will never silence her lambs.

In fact, the Hexed star encourages her sons Charles “Charlie” Bernard Foster, 28, and Christopher “Kit” Bernard Foster, 25, to explore their passions.

"Being a mother of two boys, you learn the lesson of that they have to discover things for themselves," Foster exclusively told E! The 63-year-old continued, "If they make a mistake, they make one and you just hope that they learn from it instead of intervening."

But for Foster—who shares her kids with ex Cydney Bernard—that's a parenting lesson she didn't need to pass on to her costar Hailee Steinfeld, who gave birth to daughter Harper with husband Josh Allen in April.

As the Oscar winner put it, "She is mama extraordinaire."

"It's so incredible that Hailee did this movie, first, pregnant," Foster raved, "and then holding the baby, basically."

What's more, watching Steinfeld, 29, experience many motherhood firsts brought back fond memories for Foster and their castmate Rashida Jones, who's mom to 8-year-old son Isaiah with longtime partner Ezra Koenig.

"All of the feelings and thoughts that were going through her mind at that time, realizations about her own mother and how she'd been parented, I remember that when my kids were little," Foster shared. "It's like we're all of the different eras."

But Foster also experienced a personal first while working on Hexed: playing a Disney villain.

"I've always wanted to be a villain," she said. "Now, I get to be a Disney villain, which is the most special thing, because this one is very grounded. It, in some ways, feels like somebody that you could know and weirdly, that makes it more emotional and special."

Disney's Hexed is out in theaters Nov. 25.

Hollywood CEO Paydays May Get Harder to Track as Federal Regulator Proposes Disclosure Revamp

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Fewer executives will be tracked, and less data will be shared if the SEC's proposed disclosure changes move forward.

It’s no secret that Hollywood CEO pay is often a hot-button story.

Chief execs in media, entertainment, and technology are annually among the most highly compensated people on Earth, with mid-eight-figure pay packages a common occurrence and nine-figure paydays increasingly common.

But those days might be ending soon. Not the paydays, just the disclosures, should new rules from the Securities and Exchange Commission go into effect.

The SEC on Friday proposed a revamp of its executive compensation disclosure rules, simplifying things while also changing what those disclosures will look like. The result will be that pay is disclosed for fewer executives, with only the CEO and CFO likely to be disclosed should the rule change pass (no more COO, general counsel or other C-suite pay), while the way the pay packages are disclosed shifts. Related Stories Movies 'One Sweet Hour': One Aging Elvis Impersonator. One Monk. Two Estranged Irish Brothers Facing Trauma. Movies Renny Harlin, Fixture of '90s Blockbusters, Launches Production Company Focusing on New Directors

In addition, the threshold for perquisites is being raised from $10,000 to $150,000, meaning that some perks like club memberships will no longe require disclosure, and companies will get more discretion to determine if something is a perk at all, such as CEO security costs.

At the heart of the changes are simpler disclosures, with three tables: “Earned Compensation Table, Granted Compensation Table, and Status of Equity Awards Table” with a particular emphasis on salary, bonus and equity grants.

“As applied to executive compensation disclosure, a panelist at last year’s roundtable aptly analogized current Item 402 to an ice cream parlor that carries rum raisin and maple walnut; flavors desired by only a single customer,” SEC chairman Paul Adkins said in a statement about the proposed changes. “The proposed amendments would, if adopted, revise Item 402 to focus on an objective standard regarding needs of the reasonable investor, rather than on the specialized tastes of a specific investor.”

Executives, investor relations and corporate communications teams are surely cheering the move, which will result in less gargantuan numbers than previous disclosures. Some of those changes may be warranted: After all, equity may not end up vesting if certain metrics are not achieved, and a high strike price can invalidate whatever value is in some shares. Those are things that CEOs have complained about for years: The news coverage has a headline number, but their actual take-home could be lower (or much higher!) depending on the performance of the shares.

The new disclosure structure will eliminate the paper gain spikes associated with unvested equity, and likely to be closer to what an executive really takes home, though if the old model sometimes overstated thing, the new disclosure may understate things, if the stocks consistently rise.

There will also be changes to the employee-to-CEO pay ratio, which focuses it on U.S. full-time employees and could make for more favorable comparisons.

And the changes to perquisite reporting mean that things like security, parking, country club memberships and even some private jet use could no longer be disclosed, all of which would be music to CEO ears. Even if those disclosures sometimes help tell a bigger story.

How ‘Tuner’ Sound Designer Johnnie Burn Used Dolby Atmos to Mimic Hyperacusis

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Johnnie Burn, the Oscar-winning sound designer behind “Hamnet” and “The Zone of Interest,” found Daniel Roher’s “Tuner” to be one of his most personal projects to date.

In the film, Leo Woodall plays Niki, an expert piano tuner with acutely sensitive hearing that is sometimes debilitating — in fact, it’s one reason Niki hasn’t chased his dreams of being a pianist. But it’s perfect for his profession, and he finds out that it’s perfect for safecracking too. But Niki gets in too deep with a gang of thieves as he becomes involved in a lovely relationship with gifted musician and composer Ruthie (Havana Rose Liu). Oscar-winning documentarian Daniel Roher (“Nalavany”) takes viewers on a thrill ride centered on hyperacusis, a rare condition that affects one’s hearing. Related Stories Nicolas Cage Says 'I'm a Little Bit Homeless' and 'Living in Hotels' After Sinkhole at His House Nicolas Cage Calls ‘Spider-Noir’ Season 1 'Perfect' and Says He Felt Relieved When It Was Canceled: ‘You Never Wanna Get Stuck Repeating a Character’

Burn tells Variety he experienced a mild version of hyperacusis in his late teens. “People who suffer from it chronically go through a devastating experience of not being able to possibly recover or go outside. I had this situation where I witnessed an explosion, and I went 100% deaf for about 20 minutes. When my hearing came back, I had hyperacusis and extreme sensitivity to everyday sounds.”

When Burn received the script, his first response was, “I know what this is.” His next step was researching what this world sounded like. To understand that soundscape, Burn had microphones placed inside his eardrums and went about his daily life. “I was recording things from my ears’ point of view, and playing it back later to understand what things become fairly loud that your brain would normally dial out because they’re not threatening, such as putting a pan in the dishwasher or even a shower tap — everyday sounds that can break through.”

The goal was to teach audiences how Niki interacts with his environment and what the dangers are. Burn worked to create various sound levels, including one from Niki’s point of view that allowed audiences to understand his world and another where Niki has ear defenders on, which help restrict sound. One example Burn gives is when Niki sits at a traffic light and a motorbike comes alongside him. “That’s really about teaching the audience that there are these different states in which he can be listening to things,” Burn adds. “The aim was to have the audience experience it alongside him, which is obviously with hyperacusis. It’s not like deafness or anything. It’s a situation where everyday sounds can become dangerously loud for you.”

Burn drove that home in the Dolby mix of the film, pushing the sound design. He and Roher sat in the mix, fine-tuning every decibel. “The different points of view of Niki’s hearing were a fundamental part of whether or not the film worked. I believe we cracked that. The Dolby Atmos mix was a further development of that because it gave us the ability to predominantly play sound at a much greater volume all around us.”

Everything We Know About ‘The Body’ Season 2

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Need more of The Body? The Netflix series is off to an excellent start, with fresh new characters and spooky, sexy situations that really take some turns. Here’s what we know about whether or not The Body has been renewed for season 2.

Set at a Catholic high school in the so-called innocence of 2004, the first season of The Body is about two girls who can’t express their feelings for each other, an ambitious dance team heading to regionals, and a mass hysteria that leads to prophetic visions and Satanic rituals. Totes normal, right? Once you’ve sped through season 1, you’ll be on the edge of your seat waiting to see what happens next.

As of the season 1 drop, The Body has not yet been renewed for season 2. It’s still early, however, and the buzz around the show has been good. Fingers crossed!

The Body is an original idea from Quinn Shepherd, not based on any pre-existing IP. So what happens next is entirely up to her and her team of writers and producers. Luckily, the first season ends with a cliff-hanger and a lot of unanswered questions.

“We always knew that that was where the show was going, Shepherd said in a post-mortem interview with Deadline about *that* thing with Grace. “From the beginning, that was the arc of the first season, and I think a lot of that has to do with things we would love to do next season and how to keep carrying the story.”

Executive producer Gina Gammell also mentioned Anya confessing to Father Franklin, Maddie’s prophecy, and the mystery over whether Tyler and Lisbeth actually slept together as potential avenues for season 2.

Given that the show doesn’t have a lot of movie stars with busy schedules in major roles, and filming on season 1 took place in 2025, it stands to reason that The Body could easily return in late 2027. But sometimes Netflix shows take a little longer. So again... cross your fingers and do everything short of ritual sacrifice to ensure The Body comes back for season 2 as soon as possible.

Marvel Officially Confirms New Avengers Team for Doomsday

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Avengers, New Avengers, Thunderbolts — it’s a lot to keep track of

The Marvel Cinematic Universe's Avengers lineup has been a bit muddled since the events of Captain America: Civil War, which broke up the team of besties. Things got more confusing when the team of misfits in last year's Thunderbolts* was declared the New Avengers at the end of the film, causing a bit of a kerfuffle between the team and Anthony Mackie's Sam Wilson/Captain America over who gets to call themselves Avengers.

Avengers: Doomsday is the next MCU blockbuster, and will include multiple teams of heroes squaring off against Robert Downey Jr.'s Doctor Doom. In an interview with Empire, Marvel confirmed the latest official lineup of Avengers: Sam Wilson/Captain America, Joaquin Torres/Falcon (Danny Ramirez), Scott Lang/Ant-Man (Paul Rudd), Thor (Chris Hemsworth), and Shang-Chi (Simu Liu).

Thor's a longtime Avenger, and Lang essentially joined the team during the time travel shenanigans of Avengers: Endgame. Torres has been working with Wilson for a while now, so it makes sense he'd be considered an official Avenger. Shang-Chi was introduced to Bruce Banner/Hulk (Mark Ruffalo) and Carol Danvers/Captain Marvel (Brie Larson) at the end of his solo 2021 film, so perhaps he either got an invite from Wilson between movies, or he'll be inducted as an official Avenger during the course of Doomsday.

The events of Avengers: Doomsday could not be happening at a worse time for the team. "We’re coming out of years of seemingly no organized Avengers team, and that’s exactly when they face these incursions," Marvel Studios boss Kevin Feige told Empire. "That’s always fun, to put your heroes on the back foot."

Mackie is excited to take his Captain America in a new direction. "In Endgame, Steve gave him the shield and says, 'You need to be the leader of the Avengers,'" he said. "Now you get to see more of him leading the charge with everyone into battle."

The first Avengers movie in seven years will unite old MCU favorites like Chris Evans' Steve Rogers, newcomers like the Fantastic Four, and old hats from the Fox movies like Patrick Stewart's Charles Xavier for the explosive film.

Avengers: Doomsday hits theaters Dec. 18.

‘Who’s In The Band?’ Music Competition Series From Richard Bacon, Simon Cowell & Savan Kotecha Lands Greenlight At ABC

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Music competition series Who’s In The Band? has finally scored a series order at ABC.

The Disney-owned network has officially greenlit the show, which comes from Richard Bacon, Simon Cowell and Savan Kotecha.

It marks the first new major music format for a U.S. broadcast network in some time and comes at the same time that Fox is developing a new version of Cowell’s The X Factor.

Deadline revealed that the project was in the works last year with a proof-of-concept pilot shot in London. For that taping, KPop Demon Hunters star Rei Ami as host with a panel of judges that included Joe Jonas, Mel B, and Kotecha.

Deadline understands that the deals for hosts and judges isn’t finalized but Ami is understood to remain as host. The judging panel, however, will be shaken up. Cowell was never going to be in the series, as he has a deal at NBCUniversal that precludes him from appearing on an ABC show, and Jonas is now a judge on the upcoming celebrity version of The Voice, but Mel B could still be part of the show going forward. Watch on Deadline

Who’s In The Band? is described as a “fast-paced competition built for the era of snap decisions.”

Five contestants sing the same song at once, each inside a brightly colored soundproof tube, while judges at their own mixing desks isolate vocals and eliminate hopefuls in real time. The series gives audiences an inside look at how top music industry professionals put together pop groups today, culminating in a live finale where one girl band and one boy band face a live vote.

The winners will record an album executive produced by Kotecha, who has written songs for the likes of Ariana Grande, Katy Perry, Lizzo, Britney Spears, Ed Sheeran and One Direction.

Filming will begin in January and the show will launch on ABC and Hulu in the summer of 2027.

It comes from Bacon’s Yes Yes Media, which is backed by Sister Group. Bacon devised the project and will produce alongside Syco Entertainment, Kotecha Publicis Media Content Innovation in association with Walt Disney Television Alternative.

Bacon is a well-known broadcaster in Britain, hosting shows such as Blue Peter and The Big Breakfast before moving into production. He was behind ABC’s Craig Ferguson-hosted game show The Hustler, which was produced by The Traitors producer Studio Lambert, as well as British series including This Is My House and I Literally Just Told You.

He will exec produce alongside Cowell, Kotecha and Elisabeth Murdoch. Matt Banks and Ashley Edens will serve as showrunners and will also exec produce.
